\displaystyle\lesssim-\frac{\eta|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{r\log^{2}(2+r)}-\eta\Big(1-\frac{3M}{r}\Big)^{2}\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}.

To conclude the proof, it remains to remark

|p_{t}|^{2}\,\mathds{1}_{2.5M\leq r\leq 2.7M}+\Big(1-\frac{3M}{r}\Big)^{2}\frac{|p_{u}|^{2}}{r^{2}\Omega^{4}}\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq 2.5M}\lesssim\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{r\log^{2}(2+r)}+\Big(1-\frac{3M}{r}\Big)^{2}\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}},

and to choose
\epsilon>0
small enough.
∎

Finally, in Section
6.3
, it will be convenient to simply use
p_{N}
and apply the next result. It can be proved by similar but simpler considerations than Proposition
3.4
.

Proposition 3.5
.

There holds

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(|p_{N}|\big)+4|r-3M|\frac{|p_{u}|^{2}}{M^{2}\Omega^{4}}\mathds{1}_{r\leq 2.5M}\lesssim|p_{t}|^{2}\,\mathds{1}_{2.5M\leq r\leq 2.7M}.

3.3.
Trapping weight functions

Let us introduce suitable weight functions to capture quantitatively the trapping effect of null geodesics in Schwarzschild spacetime.

We first recall some basic terminology about trapped orbits. We say that a null geodesic
\gamma
is
trapped
, if it is contained in the
trapped set

\Gamma=\big\{\,(x,p)\in\mathcal{P}\,\big|\,r-3M=p_{r^{*}}=0\,\big\}.

We also say that a null geodesic
\gamma
is
future-trapped
if
r(\gamma(s))\to 3M
as
s\to+\infty
. Similarly, we say that a null geodesic
\gamma
is
past-trapped
if
r(\gamma(s))\to 3M
as
s\to-\infty
.

3.3.1.
The trapping weight function
\varphi_{-}

Let us set the weight function that we use to capture the concentration of future-trapped geodesics. For this, we recall the vector field
G
introduced in (
4
).

Definition 3.6
.

Let
\varphi_{-}\colon\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be the weight function

\varphi_{-}(x,p)\coloneqq p(G)=\frac{r}{\Omega}p_{r^{*}}+\frac{r}{\Omega}\Big(1+\frac{6M}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{3M}{r}\Big)p_{t}.

We also introduce the rescaled weight

(22)

\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\coloneqq\Omega^{-1}\varphi_{-}.

Remark 3.7
.

The weight function
\varphi_{-}
is not
C^{1}_{x,p}
up to
{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
, moreover it vanishes there. For these reasons, we introduce the stronger weight
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}
, which also captures the redshift effect. Indeed,

•

near
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, and more generaly for
r\leq 3M
, we observe that

\displaystyle\frac{r}{\Omega}p_{r^{*}}+\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}(r-3M)p_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{r}{\Omega}(-p_{t}+p_{r^{*}})+\frac{r^{\frac{3}{2}}+|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-3M)}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}p_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{2r}{\Omega}|p_{u}|-\frac{27M^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}-|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-3M)}|p_{t}|.

We can then write

\frac{2M}{r^{2}}\frac{|p_{u}|}{\Omega^{2}}=\frac{M}{r^{3}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}+\frac{27M^{3}r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{9}{2}}-r^{3}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-3M)}|p_{t}|.

•

Near
\mathcal{I}^{+}
, and for
r\geq 3M
, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}=\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}p_{r^{*}}+\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}(r-3M)p_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2r}{\Omega^{2}}|p_{v}|+\frac{27M^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}+|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-3M)}|p_{t}|.

We now investigate the behaviour of
\varphi_{-}
along the null geodesic flow.

Lemma 3.8
.

There holds

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\varphi_{-})=-\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\varphi_{-}.

Moreover, the rescaled weight
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}
verifies

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-})=-\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-},

where
\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})
is defined as

\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\coloneqq\frac{r^{2}+2Mr+3M^{2}}{r|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{\frac{3}{2}}+M|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}})}|p_{t}|+\frac{2M|p_{u}|}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}.

Proof.

The first equality follows from
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(p_{t})=0
,

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg((r-3M)\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|r-2M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{r-3M}{2|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}|r-2M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-3M)}{2|r-2M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(r+6M)(r-2M)+(r-3M)(r-2M-r-6M)}{2|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}|r-2M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{r^{2}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}|r-2M|^{\frac{3}{2}}},

and

\displaystyle p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{r}{\Omega}p_{r^{*}}\bigg)+\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}|\not{p}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\bigg(\frac{r}{\Omega}p_{r^{*}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}(3r-6M-r)}{2|r-2M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}+\frac{r-3M}{r^{3}\Omega}|\not{p}|^{2}=\frac{r-3M}{r\Omega^{3}}|p_{t}|^{2}.

For the second equality, we write

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\Omega^{-1}\varphi_{-}\big)

\displaystyle=\Omega^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\varphi_{-}\big)+p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r}\big(\Omega^{-1}\big)\varphi_{-}

(23)

\displaystyle=\Omega^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\varphi_{-}\big)-\frac{Mp_{t}}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\Omega^{-1}\varphi_{-}+\frac{M(p_{t}-p_{r^{*}})}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\Omega^{-1}\varphi_{-}.

∎

Remark 3.9
.

The weight
\varphi_{-}
is a defining function for the submanifold
\{\varphi_{-}=0\}
of the null-shell, where past-trapped orbits are located. This property can be shown using the relations in Lemmata
3.8
and
3.10
.

3.3.2.
The trapping weight function
\varphi_{+}

The expansion phenomenon of past-trapped null geodesics can be captured by the weight function
\varphi_{+}\colon\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
, which is given by

(24)

\varphi_{+}(x,p)\coloneqq\frac{r}{\Omega}p_{r^{*}}-\frac{r}{\Omega}\Big(1+\frac{6M}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{3M}{r}\Big)p_{t}.

We note that
\varphi_{+}
is singular on
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, however
\Omega\varphi_{+}
is smooth up to
\mathcal{H}^{+}
.

Lemma 3.10
.

There holds

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\varphi_{+})

\displaystyle=\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\varphi_{+}.

Moreover, we have

\varphi_{+}\varphi_{-}=27M^{2}|p_{t}|^{2}-|\not{p}|^{2}.

Proof.

The first property can be proved in the same way as the one in Lemma
3.8
. For the second property, we simply compute

\varphi_{+}\varphi_{-}=\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}-\frac{r+6M}{r\Omega^{2}}(r-3M)^{2}|p_{t}|^{2}=-|\not{p}|^{2}+\frac{r^{3}-(r+6M)(r-3M)^{2}}{r\Omega^{2}}|p_{t}|^{2}=27M^{2}|p_{t}|^{2}-|\not{p}|^{2}.

∎

Remark 3.11
.

The weight
\varphi_{+}
is a defining function for the submanifold
\{\varphi_{+}=0\}
of
\mathcal{P}
, where future-trapped orbits are located. This property can be shown by using the relations in the Lemmata
3.8
and
3.10
.

We will rather work with the rescaled weight
\Omega\varphi_{+}
, which has the advantage of being regular up to
\mathcal{H}^{+}
and of being bounded above by
2(r+6M)|p_{t}|
. The consequence is that
\frac{\Omega|\varphi_{+}|}{2(r+6M)}
is a Lyapunov function for the null geodesic flow.

Lemma 3.12
.

The rescaled weight function
\Omega\varphi_{+}\colon\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
verifies

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\Omega\varphi_{+}\big)=\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\Omega\varphi_{+}.

Proof.

By a direct computation using Lemma
3.10
, we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\Omega\varphi_{+}\big)

\displaystyle=\Omega{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\varphi_{+}\big)+p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r}\big(\Omega\big)\varphi_{+}=\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\Omega\varphi_{+}+\frac{Mp_{t}}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\Omega\varphi_{+}-\frac{M(p_{t}-p_{r^{*}})}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\Omega\varphi_{+}.

It remains to recall from Lemma
3.8
the definition of
\boldsymbol{a}
.
∎

In what follows, we will denote the sign function by
\mathrm{sgn}
, so that
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(|g|)={\mathbb{X}}_{g}(g)\cdot\mathrm{sgn}(g)
.

Corollary 3.13
.

We have

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\bigg|\log\bigg(\frac{|\Omega\varphi_{+}|}{2(r+6M)|p_{t}|}\bigg)\bigg|\bigg)\lesssim-\frac{|p_{N}|}{r^{2}}.

Proof.

By the previous Lemma
3.12
and since
r^{2}+2Mr+3M^{2}\geq r^{2}+Mr^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\frac{|\Omega\varphi_{+}|}{r+6M}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\bigg(\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{2M|p_{u}|}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}-\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{r+6M}\bigg)\frac{|\Omega\varphi_{+}|}{r+6M}\gtrsim\bigg(\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{2}}+\frac{|p_{u}|}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\bigg)\frac{|\Omega\varphi_{+}|}{r+6M}.

Next, since
{\mathbb{X}}(2|p_{t}|)=0
, we remark that

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\bigg|\log\bigg(\frac{|\Omega\varphi_{+}|}{2(r+6M)|p_{t}|}\bigg)\bigg|\bigg)={\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\frac{|\Omega\varphi_{+}|}{r+6M}\bigg)\frac{(r+6M)}{|\Omega\varphi_{+}|}\mathrm{sgn}\bigg(\log\bigg(\frac{|\Omega\varphi_{+}|}{2(r+6M)|p_{t}|}\bigg)\bigg).

Finally, as
|\Omega\varphi_{+}|<2(r+6M)|p_{t}|
, we have that the last factor on the RHS is identically equal to
-1
. We then obtain the result from the last estimate, and
|p_{N}|\sim|p_{t}|+|p_{u}|\Omega^{-2}
.
∎

3.4.
The
r^{p}
-weight functions

We will capture the dispersion at infinity by using the weight functions
8
8

8

Note that the rescaled vector field
\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{v}
is equal to
-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}
in the coordinates
(u,x\coloneqq 1/r,\theta,\phi)
.

\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}p_{v}=p\Big(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{v}\Big),\qquad\quad\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}p_{v}=p\Big(\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{v}\Big).

Lemma 3.14
.

There holds

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}|p_{v}|\bigg)

\displaystyle=-2\frac{r-3M}{\Omega^{4}}|p_{v}|^{2}.

More generally, for all
0\leq p\leq 2
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\frac{r^{p}}{\Omega^{2}}|p_{v}|\bigg)

\displaystyle=-p\frac{r^{p-1}}{\Omega^{4}}|p_{v}|^{2}+6\frac{Mr^{p-1}}{r\Omega^{4}}|p_{v}|^{2}+(p-2)\frac{r^{p-1}}{\Omega^{2}}|p_{u}||p_{v}|.

Proof.

First, we compute

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\frac{2r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}|p_{v}|\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{2p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{r^{*}}\bigg(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\bigg)|p_{v}|-\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}|\not{p}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}(p_{r^{*}})=4\frac{r-3M}{\Omega^{4}}p_{r^{*}}|p_{v}|-\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}|\not{p}|^{2}.

We conclude the proof by using
p_{r^{*}}=p_{v}-p_{u}
, and the null-shell relation
\Omega^{2}|\not{p}|^{2}=4r^{2}p_{v}p_{u}
. For the second identity, we use
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(r^{p-2})=(p-2)p_{r^{*}}r^{p-3}
.
∎

We also show the following high-order form of the previous lemma.

Corollary 3.15
.

Let
\overline{\chi}\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
be a cutoff function such that
\overline{\chi}(s)=0
for
s\leq 4M
and
\overline{\chi}(s)=1
for
s\geq 7M
. For any
p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\overline{\chi}(r)\frac{r^{2p}}{\Omega^{2p}}|p_{v}|^{p}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p}-\frac{r^{2p-1}}{\Omega^{2p}}|p_{v}|^{p+1}\mathds{1}_{r\geq 7M}+|p_{t}|^{p+1}\mathds{1}_{4M\leq r\leq 7M},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\overline{\chi}(r)\frac{r^{2p-1}}{\Omega^{2p}}|p_{v}|^{p}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p}-\frac{r^{2p-2}}{\Omega^{2p}}|p_{v}|^{p}|p_{t}|\mathds{1}_{r\geq 7M}+|p_{t}|^{p+1}\mathds{1}_{4M\leq r\leq 7M}.

Proof.

For
r\geq 7M
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}|p_{v}|\bigg)

\displaystyle=-\frac{r-6M}{r\Omega^{4}}|p_{v}|^{2}-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}|p_{u}||p_{v}|\lesssim-|p_{t}||p_{v}|.

It then remains, in view of
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\overline{\chi}(r))=p_{r^{*}}\overline{\chi}^{\prime}(r)
and the support of
\overline{\chi}
, to combine this last estimate with Lemma
3.14
and
|p_{v}|\leq|p_{t}|
.
∎

4.
Energy boundedness and integrated energy decay estimates

Let us show the main zeroth order energy boundedness and integrated local energy decay estimates that we use. Recall that we often write
ILED
in short for integrated local energy decay estimate.

4.1.
Degenerate ILEDs

We start by proving a boundedness statement together with an ILED that only degenerates at the photon sphere and at spatial infinity.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
f
be a solution to the massless Vlasov equation
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(f)=0
. Then,

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\,\mathbb{E}\big[p_{N}f\big](\tau)+\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})}\frac{|r-3M|^{2}|p_{N}|^{2}}{r^{3}\log^{2}(2+r)}|f|+\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{r\log^{2}(2+r)}|f|+\bigg|1-\frac{3M}{r}\bigg|^{2}\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}|f|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\big[p_{N}f\big](0).

Proof.

From Proposition
3.4
, we recall the estimate for
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(|\xi|)
and that
|\xi|\sim|p_{N}|
. The result then follows from the energy estimate of Proposition
2.16
applied to
\xi f
.
∎

A similar degenerate ILED for massless Vlasov fields was previously derived in
[
Big23
, Proposition 3.4]
. In particular, the degeneracy at
\{p_{r^{*}}=0,\,r=3M\}
can be weakened (see also Lemma
6.17
). We observe that if we do not assume the finiteness of a stronger energy norm than
\mathbb{E}[p_{N}f](0)
, the degeneracy at the photon sphere is necessary as proved in
[
Big23
, Proposition 3.12]
. Nonetheless, one can remove the degeneracy at
r=3M
in the previous ILED by assuming stronger decay assumptions on the initial data.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
f
be a solution to the massless Vlasov equation. We have,

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\langle\log\bigg(\frac{|\Omega\varphi_{+}|}{r|p_{t}|}\bigg)\bigg\rangle p_{N}f\bigg](\tau)+\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})}\bigg(\frac{|p_{N}|^{2}}{r\log^{2}(2+r)}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}\bigg)|f|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\langle\log\bigg(\frac{|\Omega\varphi_{+}|}{r|p_{t}|}\bigg)\bigg\rangle p_{N}f\bigg](0).

Proof.

By Corollary
3.13
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(|\xi f|)\leq 0
, we have

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\bigg|\log\bigg(\frac{|\Omega\varphi_{+}|}{2(r+6M)|p_{t}|}\bigg)\bigg||\xi f|\bigg)\lesssim-\frac{|p_{N}|}{r^{2}}|\xi f|\lesssim-\frac{|p_{N}|^{2}}{r^{2}}|f|.

The result then follows from the energy estimate of Proposition
2.16
and from Proposition
4.1
.
∎

For the previous two ILEDs, the bulk term is degenerate with respect to the boundary term. We now prove an ILED without relative degeneration for a degenerate weighted norm.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
f
be a solution to the massless Vlasov equation
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(f)=0
. There holds,

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\,\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\big](\tau)+\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})}\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\big|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\big](0).

Proof.

It suffices to apply Proposition
2.16
to
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f
, and to compute
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f)={\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-})f
using Lemma
3.8
. Then, we use
p_{N}\sim p_{t}+\Omega^{-2}p_{u}
and
p_{N}=p_{t}
for
r\geq 2.7M
.
∎

4.2.
ILEDs for the
r^{p}
-method

We show an ILED that will be used to implement the
r^{p}
-method. We denote by
\lceil\cdot\rceil
the ceiling function.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
f
be a solution to the massless Vlasov equation. Then,

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\langle r^{p}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p}{2}\rceil}\Big\rangle\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\bigg](\tau)+\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\langle r^{p-1}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p-1}{2}\rceil}\Big\rangle\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\bigg](\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim_{p}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\langle r^{p}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p}{2}\rceil}\Big\rangle\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\bigg](0).

Proof.

Note first, according to Corollary
3.15
and using
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|)\leq 0
, that

{\mathbb{X}}\Big(\overline{\chi}(r)\frac{r^{p}}{\Omega^{2p}}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p}{2}\rceil}\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\big|\Big)\lesssim_{p}|p_{t}|\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\big|\mathds{1}_{4M\leq r\leq 7M}-r^{p-1}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p-1}{2}\rceil}|p_{v}|\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\big|\mathds{1}_{r\geq 7M}.

Let
A_{p}>0
be a sufficiently large constant and recall the parameter
R_{0}>3M
of the foliation
(\Sigma_{\tau})_{\tau\geq 0}
. As
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-})\lesssim-|p_{N}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}
and
|p_{v}|\lesssim|p_{N}|
, we have

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg[\bigg(A_{p}+r^{p}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p}{2}\rceil}\bigg)\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\big|\bigg]\lesssim_{p}-|p_{N}|\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\big|\mathds{1}_{r\leq R_{0}}-r^{p-1}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p-1}{2}\rceil}|p_{v}|\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\big|\mathds{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}.

Recall from Lemma
2.10
that
|p_{n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}}|\sim|p_{N}|
for
r\leq R_{0}
and
|p_{n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}}|=|p_{v}|
for
r\geq R_{0}
. Hence,

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg[\bigg(A_{p}+r^{p}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p}{2}\rceil}\bigg)\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\big|\bigg]\lesssim_{p}-r^{p-1}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p-1}{2}\rceil}|p_{n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}}|\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\big|.

We then deduce from the energy estimate of Proposition
2.16
and from (
coarea
) that

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\langle r^{p}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p}{2}\rceil}\Big\rangle\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\bigg](\tau)+\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[r^{p-1}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p-1}{2}\rceil}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\bigg](\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim_{p}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\langle r^{p}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p}{2}\rceil}\Big\rangle\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f\bigg](0).

The result then ensues by using also this estimate for
p=1
.
∎

Remark 4.5
.

The ILED of Proposition
4.4
can be extended to the more general case when
p\geq 0
instead of
p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
(see
[
Big23
, Section 4]
).

Finally, we prove a slight generalisation of Proposition
4.4
. We will apply it to the system of the commuted Vlasov equation that we consider in Section
6
. The individual derivatives that we will consider do not satisfy the Vlasov equation. However, we will circumvent this difficulty by considering a well-chosen weighted combination of them.

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
n\geq 1
. Let further
g_{k}\colon\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
, with
1\leq k\leq n
, be sufficiently regular distribution functions such that

(25)

\sum_{1\leq k\leq n}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(|g_{k}|)\lesssim-\sum_{1\leq k\leq n}|p_{N}||g_{k}|\mathds{1}_{r\leq 7M}.

Then, for any
p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we have

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\sum_{1\leq k\leq n}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\langle r^{p}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p}{2}\rceil}\Big\rangle g_{k}\Big](\tau)+\sum_{1\leq k\leq n}\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\langle r^{p-1}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p-1}{2}\rceil}\Big\rangle g_{k}\Big](\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim_{p}\sum_{1\leq k\leq n}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\langle r^{p}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p}{2}\rceil}\Big\rangle g_{k}\Big](0).

Proof.

It suffices to follow the proof of Proposition
4.4
by formally replacing
|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}f|
by
\sum_{k}|g_{k}|
.
∎

5.
Commutation vector fields

Let us introduced a well-chosen class of commutation vector fields that will be used in the article.

5.1.
The complete lifts of the Killing vector fields

For any (conformal) Killing vector field
X\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{S})
, the associated complete lift
\widehat{X}\in\Gamma(TT^{*}\mathcal{S})
is tangent to
\mathcal{P}
, and commutes with the Vlasov operator
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}
. In fact,
\widehat{X}={\mathbb{X}}_{p(X)}
, the symplectic gradient of the conserved quantity
p(X)
. This result holds in a general Lorentzian manifold. See
[
AnCGS22
, Appendix F]
and
[
FJS17
, Appendix C]
for more information. We restrict our discussion to the case of a Schwarzschild background.

Definition 5.1
.

The complete lifts of the Killing vector fields of Schwarzschild spacetime are

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{1}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,-\sin\phi\,\partial_{\theta}-\cot\theta\cos\phi\,\partial_{\phi}-\cos\phi\frac{p_{\phi}}{\sin^{2}\theta}\partial_{p_{\theta}}+(\cos\phi\,p_{\theta}-\sin\phi\cot\theta\,p_{\phi})\partial_{p_{\phi}},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{2}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,\cos\phi\,\partial_{\theta}-\cot\theta\sin\phi\partial_{\phi}-\sin\phi\frac{p_{\phi}}{\sin^{2}\theta}\partial_{p_{\theta}}+(\sin\phi\,p_{\theta}+\cos\phi\cot\theta\,p_{\phi})\partial_{p_{\phi}},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{3}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\,\partial_{\phi},\qquad\qquad\widehat{\partial}_{t}=\partial_{t}.

The time derivative and the angular derivatives of the energy-momentum tensor
\mathrm{T}[f]
of a massless Vlasov field
f
can then be estimated using the next result.

Proposition 5.2
.

For any
\widehat{Z}\in\big\{\partial_{t},\,\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{1},\,\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{2},\,\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{3}\big\}
, we have
[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\widehat{Z}]=0
. Moreover, there holds

\mathcal{L}_{Z}\big(\mathrm{T}[f]\big)=\mathrm{T}\big[\widehat{Z}f\big].

It will be convenient to work with the following commuting vector fields associated to the total angular momentum
|\not{p}|
. Recall from (
20
) the Carter tensor
Q
.

Definition 5.3
.

Let
{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{P})
be the projection of the symplectic gradient
\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|^{2}}
. In local coordinates,

{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}\coloneqq p_{\theta}\partial_{\theta}+\frac{p_{\phi}}{\sin^{2}\theta}\partial_{\phi}+\frac{\cot\theta}{\sin^{2}\theta}p_{\phi}^{2}\partial_{p_{\theta}}.

In order to work with a vector field with the same homogeneity in
p
as the other vector fields we will manipulate, we also introduce

{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}=|\not{p}|^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}.

Corollary 5.4
.

There holds
{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}=\sum_{i}p(\mathbf{\Omega}_{i})\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{i}
. In particular, we have
[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}]=[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}]=0
.

Note that the radial derivative does not generate a symmetry of Schwarzschild spacetime. Consequently, controlling
\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{r}}\mathrm{T}[f]
requires a more thorough analysis.

5.2.
The radial scaling vector fields

We investigate further the structure of the massless Vlasov operator. We focus on the properties of the time-radial part
\boldsymbol{R}\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{P})
and radial part
R\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{P})
of
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}
, given by

(26)

\boldsymbol{R}\coloneqq-\frac{p_{t}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{t}+\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{r^{*}}+\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}|\not{p}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}},\qquad\qquad R\coloneqq\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{r^{*}}+\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}|\not{p}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

In this relativistic setting, it is more natural to work with
\boldsymbol{R}
. We note that contrary to
R
, the vector field
\boldsymbol{R}
is regular up to
{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
. Nonetheless, as
\boldsymbol{R}-R
is a multiple of
\partial_{t}
, which commutes with
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}
, focusing on
R
will sometimes be convenient.

Lemma 5.5
.

The following decomposition holds

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}=\boldsymbol{R}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}.

Note that if
f
is solution to the massless Vlasov equation
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(f)=0
, Lemma
5.5
together with Corollary
5.4
allow to prove boundedness for
r^{2}\boldsymbol{R}f
. Unfortunately, this does not allow to get a satisfying estimate for
\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{r}}\mathrm{T}[f]
. In order to obtain more information on this last quantity, let us exhibit a scaling vector field in the span of
\{\partial_{r^{*}},\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\}
that commutes well with
R
. Let
L\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{P})
be the vector field defined by

L\coloneqq g(r)\partial_{r}+p_{r^{*}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}},\qquad\quad g(r)\coloneqq\frac{1}{27M^{2}}r(r-3M)(r+6M).

Proposition 5.6
.

There holds

(27)

[R,L]=\frac{(r-3M)(r+5M)}{9M^{2}}R.

Proof.

We will prove that
L
is the unique vector field of the form
g(r)\partial_{r}+p_{r^{*}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}
that is not singular at the photon sphere and having a commutation of the form (
27
) with
R
. For this, we first note that

\displaystyle[R,L]

\displaystyle=p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r}g(r)\partial_{r}+\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}|\not{p}|^{2}\partial_{p^{*}}-\bigg(g(r)\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}\bigg)|\not{p}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\big(\partial_{r}g(r)-1\big)p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r}+\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}-g(r)\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}\bigg)\bigg)|\not{p}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

Then,
[R,L]
is collinear to
R
if and only if
g
verifies

g(r)\partial_{r}\left(\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}\right)-\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}=\big(1-\partial_{r}g(r)\big)\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}},

which is equivalent to

\partial_{r}\left(g(r)\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}\right)=2\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}.

Integrating this last property and using that
g
is continuous, we get

g(r)\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}=2\int_{3M}^{r}\frac{s-3M}{s^{4}}\mathrm{d}s=-\frac{1}{r^{2}}+\frac{2M}{r^{3}}+\frac{1}{27M^{2}}=\frac{(r-3M)^{2}(r+6M)}{27M^{2}r^{3}}.

It gives us the expression of
g(r)
, from which we can compute
\partial_{r}g(r)-1
and conclude the proof.
∎

It will be convenient to add a vector field proportional to
\partial_{t}
to
L
in order to improve its commutation properties with
\boldsymbol{R}
. Let
\boldsymbol{L}\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{P})
be given by

(28)

\boldsymbol{L}\coloneqq-\frac{g(r)p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\partial_{t}+L.

Proposition 5.7
.

There holds

[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\boldsymbol{L}]=\frac{(r-3M)(r+5M)}{9M^{2}}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}-\frac{(r-3M)(r+3M)}{27M^{2}r^{2}}{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}+\frac{4(r-3M)(r+3M)p_{u}p_{v}}{27M^{2}\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\partial_{t}.

Proof.

Recall
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}=-\Omega^{-2}p_{t}\partial_{t}+R+r^{-2}{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}
and note that
[{\mathbb{X}}_{Q},L]=[\partial_{t},L]=0
. Hence, by Proposition
5.6
,

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},L]

\displaystyle=[R,L]+L\Big(\,\frac{p_{t}}{\Omega^{2}}\,\Big)\partial_{t}+2\frac{g(r)}{r^{3}}{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}

\displaystyle=\frac{(r-3M)(r+5M)}{9M^{2}}R+2\frac{g(r)}{r^{3}}{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}+\bigg(-g(r)\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}-\frac{2Mg(r)p_{t}}{r^{2}\Omega^{4}}+\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\bigg)\partial_{t}.

Moreover, as
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(p_{t})=0
and
[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\partial_{t}]=0
, we have

\Big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},-\frac{g(r)p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\partial_{t}\Big]=-\frac{g(r)(r-3M)|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{4}\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\partial_{t}+\frac{2Mg(r)|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{r^{2}\Omega^{4}p_{t}}\partial_{t}-\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\partial_{r}g(r)\partial_{t}.

We now write
R={\mathbb{X}}_{g}+p_{t}\Omega^{-2}\partial_{t}-r^{-2}{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}
, and use the relations

\partial_{r}g(r)-1=\frac{(r-3M)(r+5M)}{9M^{2}},\qquad\quad\Omega^{2}|\not{p}|^{2}=r^{2}\big(|p_{t}|^{2}-|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}\big),

to get

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\boldsymbol{L}]

\displaystyle=\frac{(r-3M)(r+5M)}{9M^{2}}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}-\frac{(r-3M)(r+3M)}{27M^{2}}{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}

\displaystyle\quad-\bigg(2g(r)\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}\Omega^{4}}+\frac{2Mg(r)}{r^{2}\Omega^{4}}-\frac{(r-3M)(r+5M)}{9M^{2}\Omega^{2}}\bigg)\frac{|p_{t}|^{2}-|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{p_{t}}\partial_{t},

from which we deduce the result.
∎

Remark 5.8
.

The vector field
\boldsymbol{L}
has to be compared with a scaling vector field used in
[
VRVR24
]
for the study of the linearised system
\mathbb{X}_{\mathrm{lin}}(f)=0
(recall Subsection
1.5
) and an associated non-linear problem. It is closely related to the vector fields
V_{\pm}
that we use to deal with trapping (see Proposition
5.14
below).

5.3.
Trapping vector fields

Let us introduce some projections on
T\mathcal{P}
parallel to
\partial_{p_{t}}
of symplectic gradients that arise from the unstable and stable trapping effects. We refer to Appendix
B
for more details.

We first introduce
V_{+}\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{P})
, the projection of
{\mathbb{X}}_{\varphi_{-}}
, which is given in coordinates by

V_{+}\coloneqq\partial_{p_{t}}(\varphi_{-})\partial_{t}+\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}(\varphi_{-})\partial_{r^{*}}-\partial_{r^{*}}(\varphi_{-})\partial_{p_{r^{*}}},

where
\partial_{p_{t}}\varphi_{-}
has to be understood as a quantity defined on
T^{*}\mathcal{S}
.

Proposition 5.9
.

In local coordinates, we have

(29)

V_{+}=\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}(r-3M)\partial_{t}+\frac{r}{\Omega}\partial_{r^{*}}-\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r\Omega}p_{r^{*}}+\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}p_{t}\bigg)\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

Proof.

The quantities
\partial_{p_{t}}\varphi_{-}
and
\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\varphi_{-}
can be easily computed from the expression of
\varphi_{-}
, introduced in Definition
3.6
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{r^{*}}(\varphi_{-})

\displaystyle=\Omega^{2}\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-3M)}{|r-2M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}p_{r^{*}}+\Omega^{2}\frac{r^{2}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}|r-2M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}p_{t}=\frac{(r-3M)}{r\Omega}p_{r^{*}}+\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}p_{t}.

∎

Remark 5.10
.

It will sometimes be convenient to use an alternative expression for
V_{+}
by rewriting the last term of (
29
) using

(30)

\frac{r-3M}{r\Omega}p_{r^{*}}+\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}p_{t}=\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}}\varphi_{-}+\frac{27M^{2}\Omega}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}p_{t}.

We now compute two commutators that will be useful to determine
[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},V_{+}]
.

Lemma 5.11
.

There holds

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\partial_{r^{*}}]

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{(r-3M)p_{t}}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}+\frac{(r-3M)|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\bigg)\partial_{t}-2\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r^{*}}+\Omega^{2}\frac{r-6M}{r^{5}}|\not{p}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+2\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r}{\mathbb{X}}_{g},

\displaystyle\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\partial_{t}-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{r^{*}}.

Proof.

We first recall the definition (
26
) of
\boldsymbol{R}
and Lemma
5.5
. We have

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}=\boldsymbol{R}+r^{-2}{\mathbb{X}}_{Q},\qquad\qquad[{\mathbb{X}}_{Q},\partial_{r^{*}}]=[{\mathbb{X}}_{Q},\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}]=0.

The second commutator can then be obtained from (
16
) and (
18
). For the first identity, we compute

\partial_{r^{*}}\big(\Omega^{-2}\big)=-\frac{2M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}},\quad\qquad\partial_{r^{*}}\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}\bigg)=-3\Omega^{2}\frac{r-4M}{r^{5}},\quad\qquad\partial_{r^{*}}\big(r^{-2}\big)=-2\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r}r^{-2},

which can be obtained using
\Omega^{2}=1-\frac{2M}{r}
and
\partial_{r^{*}}=\Omega^{2}\partial_{r}
. Moreover, we also have

(31)

\partial_{r^{*}}(p_{t})=\partial_{r^{*}}\bigg(\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{2}}\bigg)\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2p_{t}}=-\Omega^{2}\frac{(r-3M)|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{4}p_{t}},

by using again (
16
). We then deduce that

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\partial_{r^{*}}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{(r-3M)|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{4}p_{t}}\partial_{t}-\frac{2M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}p_{t}\partial_{t}+\frac{2M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r^{*}}+3\Omega^{2}\frac{r-4M}{r^{5}}|\not{p}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\quad+2\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}+\frac{2}{r}p_{t}\partial_{t}-\frac{2}{r}p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r^{*}}-2\Omega^{2}\frac{r-3M}{r^{5}}|\not{p}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

It remains to use the null-shell relation
\Omega^{2}|\not{p}|^{2}=r^{2}p_{t}^{2}-r^{2}p_{r^{*}}^{2}
.
∎

We can then compute
[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},V_{+}]
. One can check that the coefficient of
V_{+}
in the error term has a good sign. This suggests that
V_{+}
is a good vector field for the analysis of massless Vlasov fields.

Proposition 5.12
.

There holds

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},V_{+}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}V_{+}-\frac{r+3M}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}|p_{t}|\varphi_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+2\Omega{\mathbb{X}}_{g}.

Proof.

We will work with the expression of
V_{+}
given by Remark
5.10
. Since
[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\partial_{t}]=0
,
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(p_{t})=0
and
\partial_{r^{*}}=\Omega^{2}\partial_{r}
, we have

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},V_{+}]

\displaystyle=p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}(r-3M)\bigg)\partial_{t}+p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{r}{\Omega}\bigg)\partial_{r^{*}}-p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}}\bigg)\varphi_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}-\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\varphi_{-})\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\quad-p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{27M^{2}\Omega}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\bigg)p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+\frac{r}{\Omega}[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\partial_{r^{*}}]-\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}}\varphi_{-}+\frac{27M^{2}\Omega}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{3}{2}}}p_{t}\bigg)\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\big].

From Lemma
5.11
, we can infer that

[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},V_{+}]=a_{t}\partial_{t}+a_{r^{*}}\partial_{r^{*}}+a_{p_{r^{*}}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+2\Omega{\mathbb{X}}_{g},

where
a_{x}
are smooth functions of
r
,
p_{t}
,
p_{r^{*}}
and
|\not{p}|^{2}
. Let us determine
a_{t}
. As
r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega=|r-2M|^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}(r-3M)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|r-2M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{r-3M}{2|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}|r-2M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{(r-3M)|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2|r-2M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(r+6M)(r-2M)+(r-3M)(r-2M-r-6M))}{2|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}|r-2M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{r^{2}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}|r-2M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

Thus, we get using first Lemma
5.11
and then relation (
30
) that

\displaystyle a_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{r^{2}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}|r-2M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}p_{r^{*}}+\frac{(r-3M)p_{t}}{r\Omega^{3}}+\frac{(r-3M)|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{r\Omega^{3}p_{t}}-\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}}\varphi_{-}+\frac{27M^{2}\Omega}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{3}{2}}}p_{t}\bigg)\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(r-3M)p_{t}}{r\Omega^{3}}=-\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\cdot\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}(r-3M).

Applying once again Lemma
5.11
and the relation (
30
), we obtain

\displaystyle a_{r^{*}}

\displaystyle=p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{r}{\Omega}\bigg)-2\frac{r-3M}{r\Omega^{3}}p_{r^{*}}+\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r\Omega}p_{r^{*}}+\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}p_{t}\bigg)=-\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\cdot\frac{r}{\Omega}.

We recall from Lemma
3.8
the expression of
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\varphi_{-})
. We then have to prove that

a_{p_{r^{*}}}+\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\varphi_{-})-\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\cdot\frac{27M^{2}\Omega}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{3}{2}}}p_{t}=-\frac{r+3M}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}|p_{t}|\varphi_{-}.

For this, we compute

\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}}\bigg)=-\frac{r-6M}{r^{3}},\qquad\quad\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{27M^{2}\Omega}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{3}{2}}}\bigg)=-\frac{54M^{2}(r^{2}+2Mr-12M^{2})}{|r-2M|^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}r^{3}}.

Hence, by Lemma
5.11
, we have

a_{p_{r^{*}}}+\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\varphi_{-})=\frac{r-6M}{r^{3}}\varphi_{-}p_{r^{*}}+\frac{54M^{2}(r^{2}+2Mr-12M^{2})}{|r-2M|^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}r^{3}}p_{r^{*}}p_{t}+\Omega\frac{r-6M}{r^{4}}|\not{p}|^{2}.

Moreover, by the null-shell relation there holds

\Omega\frac{r-6M}{r^{4}}|\not{p}|^{2}=\frac{r-6M}{r^{2}\Omega}(|p_{t}|^{2}-|p_{r^{*}}|^{2})=\frac{r-6M}{r^{2}\Omega}|p_{t}|^{2}-\frac{r-6M}{r^{3}}\varphi_{-}p_{r^{*}}+\frac{(r-6M)(r-3M)|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{7}{2}}\Omega}p_{t}p_{r^{*}}.

We further remark that

\displaystyle\frac{r-6M}{r^{2}\Omega}|p_{t}|^{2}-\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\cdot\frac{27M^{2}\Omega}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{3}{2}}}p_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{r^{2}-9M^{2}}{r^{2}(r+6M)\Omega}|p_{t}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{r+3M}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}p_{t}\varphi_{-}-\frac{r+3M}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega}p_{t}p_{r^{*}}.

The result then ensues from

\frac{(r-6M)(r-3M)|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{7}{2}}\Omega}-\frac{r+3M}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega}=\frac{(r-6M)(r-3M)|r+6M|^{2}-(r+3M)r^{3}}{r^{\frac{7}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega},

and the relation

(r-6M)(r-3M)|r+6M|^{2}-(r+3M)r^{3}=-54M^{2}r^{2}-108M^{3}r+54\cdot 12M^{4}.

∎

Let us also consider the projection on
T\mathcal{P}
parallel to
\partial_{p_{t}}
of the symplectic gradient
{\mathbb{X}}_{\varphi_{+}}
associated to the weight
\varphi_{+}
. It is given by

V_{-}=-\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}(r-3M)\partial_{t}+\frac{r}{\Omega}\partial_{r^{*}}-\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r\Omega}p_{r^{*}}-\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}p_{t}\bigg)\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

Even if we will not use this vector field in the proof of Theorem
1.1
, we state the next two propositions for completeness.

Proposition 5.13
.

We have

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},V_{-}]

\displaystyle=\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}V_{-}+\frac{r+3M}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}|p_{t}|\varphi_{+}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+2\Omega{\mathbb{X}}_{g}.

Proof.

The computations are similar to the ones carried out during the proof of Proposition
5.12
.
∎

Finally, we obtain some identities relating the radial part of the Vlasov operator
\boldsymbol{R}={\mathbb{X}}_{g}-r^{-2}{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}
, the radial scaling vector field
\boldsymbol{L}
and
V_{\pm}
.

Proposition 5.14
.

The following relations hold,

\displaystyle\varphi_{-}V_{-}+\varphi_{+}V_{+}

\displaystyle=2r^{2}\boldsymbol{R}+54M^{2}p_{t}\partial_{t},

\displaystyle\varphi_{-}V_{-}-\varphi_{+}V_{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{54M^{2}p_{t}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\boldsymbol{L}.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\varphi_{+}V_{+}+\varphi_{-}V_{-}

\displaystyle=\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}(r-3M)(\varphi_{+}-\varphi_{-})\partial_{t}+\frac{r}{\Omega}(\varphi_{+}+\varphi_{-})\partial_{r^{*}}

\displaystyle\quad-\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r\Omega}p_{r^{*}}(\varphi_{+}+\varphi_{-})+\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}p_{t}(\varphi_{+}-\varphi_{-})\bigg)\partial_{p_{r^{*}}},

and

\varphi_{+}+\varphi_{-}=2\frac{rp_{r^{*}}}{\Omega},\quad\qquad\varphi_{+}-\varphi_{-}=-2\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}(r-3M)p_{t}.

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{+}V_{+}+\varphi_{-}V_{-}

\displaystyle=-2\frac{r+6M}{r\Omega^{2}}(r-3M)^{2}p_{t}\partial_{t}+2\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r^{*}}+2\frac{r-3M}{\Omega^{2}}(|p_{t}|^{2}-|p_{r^{*}}|^{2})\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

Similarly, one has

\varphi_{+}V_{+}-\varphi_{-}V_{-}=2\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\Omega^{2}}(r-3M)p_{r^{*}}\partial_{t}-2\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\Omega^{2}}(r-3M)p_{t}\partial_{r^{*}}-2\frac{27M^{2}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}p_{r^{*}}p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

Finally, recall the null-shell relation
\frac{|p_{t}|^{2}-|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}=\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{2}}
as well as the definitions (
26
) and (
28
) of
\boldsymbol{R}
and
\boldsymbol{L}
.
∎

5.4.
The improved commutation vector field

The commutation formula for
V_{+}
is not completely satisfying because of the error term proportional to
\varphi_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}
. While one can expect the energy flux of
\varphi_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f
to be bounded in
L^{1}(\pi^{-1}(\Sigma_{\tau}))
, we will avoid controlling a non-degenerate bulk norm for this quantity in view of the discussion of Section
1.5.3
(recall in particular (
13
)). For this purpose, we modify
V_{+}
with a vector field proportional to
\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}
. The correction will compensate for the bad error term.

5.4.1.
Some properties of the vector field
\varphi_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}

It turns out that one obtains a simpler commutation formula by rescaling
\varphi_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}
by
r\Omega^{-1}
. For this reason, we recall
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\coloneqq\Omega^{-1}\varphi_{-}
.

Proposition 5.15
.

There holds

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}]

\displaystyle=\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{2}}{p_{t}}\partial_{t}-\frac{\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}}{\Omega}V_{+}.

Remark 5.16
.

Contrary to
r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}
, the vector field
r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}
is not regular up to the future event horizon.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.11
and Proposition
5.9
, giving the expression of
V_{+}
in local coordinates,

[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}]=\frac{\varphi_{-}}{r\Omega}\partial_{t}-\frac{p_{t}}{r\Omega}V_{+}-\frac{p_{t}}{r\Omega}\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r\Omega}p_{r^{*}}+\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}p_{t}\bigg)\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

We then obtain from Lemma
3.8
that

[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\varphi_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}]=\frac{|\varphi_{-}|^{2}}{r\Omega p_{t}}\partial_{t}-\frac{\varphi_{-}}{r\Omega}V_{+}-\frac{(r-3M)p_{r^{*}}}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\varphi_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

Finally, we get the result from
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}=\Omega^{-1}\varphi_{-}
and

\partial_{r}(r\Omega^{-1})=\Omega^{-1}-\frac{M}{r}\Omega^{-3}=\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}r\Omega^{-1}.

∎

We note that one cannot control a non-degenerate bulk integral of
r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}p_{N}p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f
near
{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
by simply using the previous commutation formula and an energy estimate. Indeed, in view of Remark
5.16
, one cannot use the redshift weight in order to generate a good error term because of regularity issues. To circumvent this difficulty, we will use the next estimate.

Lemma 5.17
.

Let
f
be a solution the massless Vlasov equation. For
r<2.7M
, there holds

\bigg|\frac{2|p_{u}|}{\Omega^{2}}p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f-\frac{rp_{r^{*}}}{(r-3M)}\Omega^{-1}V_{+}f\bigg|\lesssim\big|p_{N}\partial_{t}f\big|+|\not{p}|\big|{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f\big|+\big(|p_{t}|^{2}+|\not{p}|^{2}\big)\big|\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|.

Proof.

As
p_{t}=2p_{v}-p_{r^{*}}
, in view of the null-shell relation
4r^{2}|p_{u}||p_{v}|=\Omega^{2}|\not{p}|^{2}
, it suffices to estimate
\frac{2|p_{u}|}{\Omega^{2}}p_{r^{*}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f
. We write

\displaystyle\frac{2p_{u}}{\Omega^{2}}\frac{r-3M}{r}p_{r^{*}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{r-3M}{r\Omega^{2}}|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}-\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}p_{r^{*}}p_{t}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}p_{r^{*}}p_{t}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+\frac{r-3M}{r\Omega^{2}}p_{r^{*}}p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

We remark now that

\displaystyle\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}p_{r^{*}}p_{t}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+\frac{r-3M}{r\Omega^{2}}p_{r^{*}}p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}=\frac{27M^{2}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{\frac{3}{2}}-|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-3M))}p_{t}p_{r^{*}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}},

and

\displaystyle-\frac{r-3M}{r\Omega^{2}}|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}-\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}p_{r^{*}}p_{t}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}=\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega}V_{+}-p_{r^{*}}\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}(r-3M)\partial_{t}-p_{r^{*}}\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{r^{*}}.

Next, we use

\displaystyle\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r^{*}}=r\big({\mathbb{X}}_{g}-r^{-2}{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}\big)+\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}p_{t}\partial_{t}-\frac{r-3M}{r^{3}}|\not{p}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}},

and we observe that

\displaystyle\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}(r-3M)p_{r^{*}}\partial_{t}+\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}p_{t}\partial_{t}=\frac{2rp_{u}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{t}+\frac{27M^{2}r}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{\frac{3}{2}}-|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-3M))}p_{r^{*}}\partial_{t}.

The last five equalities imply the result since
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(f)=0
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}=|\not{p}|{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}
.
∎

5.4.2.
The modified vector field
V_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}

Let us now define the modification of the vector field
V_{+}
that we will use to perform integrated energy estimates without relative degeneration.

Definition 5.18
.

Let
V_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{P})
be the vector field

V_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}\coloneqq V_{+}+\Omega\Phi r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}},

where
\Phi
is uniquely determined by

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\Omega\Phi)+\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}(\Omega\Phi)=\frac{r+3M}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}|p_{t}|\Omega,\qquad\quad(\Omega\Phi)|_{t^{*}=0}=0.

Proposition 5.19
.

There holds

\displaystyle\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},V_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}\big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}V_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}+\frac{\Omega\Phi|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{2}}{p_{t}}\partial_{t}-\Phi\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}V_{+}+2\Omega{\mathbb{X}}_{g}.

Proof.

We write first

\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},V_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}\big]=[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},V_{+}]+\Omega\Phi[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}]+{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\Omega\Phi)r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

Next, recall the commutation formulae in Propositions
5.12
and
5.15
. Finally, we use the definition of
\Phi
.
∎

In order to use the vector field
V_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}
, we have to estimate
\Phi
as well as the derivative
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Phi
.

Proposition 5.20
.

There holds

\sup_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}\geq 0\})}\,\frac{|p_{N}|+|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{|p_{t}|}\big|r\Phi\big|+\frac{|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{3}{4}}}{|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}}\big|r\Phi\big|+\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Phi\big|<+\infty.

The proof of Proposition
5.20
is performed in Appendix
A
(see Proposition
A.1
and Remark
A.2
).

5.5.
The rescaled trapping vector fields

In order to exploit the redshift effect and obtain a stronger control of massless Vlasov fields near
{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
, we will work with
\Omega^{-1}V_{+}
which does not vanish there. This vector field is smooth up to the future event horizon.

Lemma 5.21
.

Let
\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\coloneqq\Omega^{-1}V_{+}
and recall the definition of
\boldsymbol{a}
from Lemma
3.8
. There holds

\displaystyle\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\big]

\displaystyle=-\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}-\frac{r+3M}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|p_{t}|\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+2{\mathbb{X}}_{g}.

Proof.

We have

\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\big]=\Omega^{-1}[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},V_{+}]+p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r}\big(\Omega^{-1}\big)V_{+}.

To get the result, it remains to use the commutation formula of Proposition
5.12
and to perform the same computations as in the proof of Lemma
3.8
, which lead to

(32)

\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})=\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}-p_{r^{*}}\partial_{r}\big(\Omega^{-1}\big).

∎

Similarly, we can work with a regularised version of
V_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}
, allowing for a better control of
f
near
\mathcal{H}^{+}
.

Corollary 5.22
.

Let
\boldsymbol{V}_{+}^{\mathrm{mod}}\coloneqq\Omega^{-1}V_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}
, and let
f
be a solution to the massless Vlasov equation. Then,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f\big|\big)+\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f\big|\lesssim\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{r^{2}}\big|\partial_{t}f\big|+\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}}{r^{\frac{7}{4}}}\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}f|.

Proof.

We use the commutation formula in Proposition
5.19
to get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\big|V_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f\big|\big)+\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\big|V_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f\big|\lesssim\Omega\frac{|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}||r\Phi|}{|p_{t}|}\cdot\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{r^{2}}\big|\partial_{t}f\big|+\frac{|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|r\Phi|}{|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}}\cdot\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}}{r^{\frac{7}{4}}}\big|V_{+}f\big|.

We conclude the proof by using (
32
) and Proposition
5.20
.
∎

It turns out that in order to control
|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}f|
, we will need to study a well-chosen derivative of
f
collinear to
\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}
.

Definition 5.23
.

Let
\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}\!\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{P})
be the vector field given by

\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}\coloneqq\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{5}{4}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

Remark 5.24
.

We have
\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{5}{4}}\lesssim|p_{t}||p_{N}|
, so that
\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}
is regular up to
{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
.

We conclude this section with a consequence of Lemma
5.17
.

Corollary 5.25
.

Let
f
be a solution the massless Vlasov equation. For
r<2.7M
, we have

\bigg|\frac{|p_{u}|}{\Omega^{2}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\bigg|\lesssim|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\Omega^{2}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}f\big|+\big|p_{N}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\big|+\big|p_{N}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f\big|+\bigg(|p_{t}|+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{|p_{t}|}\bigg)\big|\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|.

Proof.

We use the estimate of Lemma
5.17
, multiplied by
\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}|p_{t}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{5}{4}}
. We conclude the proof by using
\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq 3|p_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}
.
∎

6.
Integrated energy decay estimates without relative degeneration

We introduce a well-chosen energy flux
\mathcal{E}[f]
to show decay for massless Vlasov fields on Schwarzschild. Using this energy flux, we prove an integrated local energy decay estimates without relative degeneration.

6.1.
Energy norms and statement of the main results

Motivated by the discussion of Section
1.5
(recall in particular Steps
3
and
4
), we introduce the following first order energy fluxes.

Definition 6.1
.

We set, for any distribution function
g:\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
, the first order energy flux

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[g]

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}g\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}g\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[p_{N}\,\boldsymbol{V}_{+}^{\mathrm{mod}}g\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}g\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}g\big].

Let further

\mathcal{E}[g]\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[p_{N}g]+\mathcal{F}[g].

The main result proved in this section can be stated as follows.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
f
be a solution to the massless Vlasov equation such that
\mathcal{E}[f](0)<+\infty
. There exists
C>0
, depending only on
M
, such that

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\,\mathcal{E}[f](\tau)+\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\mathcal{E}\big[r^{-1}\log^{-2}(2+r)f\big](\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq C\mathcal{E}[f](0).

For the proof of this proposition we will first show a similar result for the weaker norm
\mathcal{F}[f]
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
f
be a solution to the massless Vlasov equation such that
\mathcal{F}[f](0)<+\infty
. There exists
C>0
, depending only on
M
, such that

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\,\mathcal{F}[f](\tau)+\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\mathcal{F}\big[r^{-1}\log^{-2}(2+r)f\big](\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq C\mathcal{F}[f](0).

We fix, for the remainder of this section
6
, a solution
f
to
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(f)=0
verifying
\mathcal{F}[f](0)<+\infty
. Since
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\partial_{t}f)={\mathbb{X}}_{g}({\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f)=0
, we get from Proposition
4.3
,
|p_{n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}}|\lesssim|p_{N}|
and (
coarea
) that, for all
\tau\geq 0
,

(33)

\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\big](\tau)+\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f\big](\tau)+\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\!\mathbb{E}\big[r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\big](\tau)+\mathbb{E}\big[r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f\big](\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim\mathcal{F}[f](0).\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\square\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Consequently, we only need to control the last three fluxes constituting
\mathcal{F}[f]
. We will proceed as follows.

(a)

In Section
6.2
, we control the derivatives of
f
in a favourable region that can be treated independently of the rest of the null-shell. This is explained by the fact that no future-directed null geodesic can enter this domain.

(b)

In Section
6.3
, we will control the two degenerate derivatives
|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}f
and
\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f
.

(c)

In Section
6.4
, we conclude the proof of Proposition
6.3
by estimating
p_{N}\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f
.

(d)

In Section
6.5
, we control
f
using the norm
\mathcal{E}[f]
. This will show Proposition
6.2
.

6.2.
The far-away incoming particles case

We begin controlling the Vlasov field
f
in the region where
r\gg 2M
and
p_{r^{*}}<0
. In this region, motivated by the Minkowskian case, we will use the vector field

S\coloneqq\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{t}+\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{r^{*}}-p_{r^{*}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

We will prove the next estimate.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
R\geq 832M
. There exists
C>0
such that for all
\tau\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[p_{t}|Sf|\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\Big](\tau)+\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\Big[p_{t}\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\Big](\tau)

\displaystyle+\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})\cap\{r\geq R,\,p_{r^{*}}\leq 0\}}\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{(r+2)\log^{2}(2+r)}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{4r^{3}}\bigg)\Big(\big|Sf\big|+\frac{1}{4}\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\Big){\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 4\mathbb{E}\Big[p_{t}|Sf|\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\Big](0)+\mathbb{E}\Big[p_{t}\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\Big](0)+C\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\big](0)+C\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f\big](0).

Remark 6.5
.

This energy estimate allows to control
|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}f
and
D_{p_{r^{*}}}f
in
\{r\geq R,\,p_{r^{*}}\leq 0\}
. As
R\geq 4M
and
p_{r^{*}}\leq 0
, we have
|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|\sim r|p_{t}|
on this region, so

\big|p_{t}Sf\big|+|p_{t}|\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\lesssim\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\big|+|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}f\big|+\big|\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|

and

|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}f\big|+\big|p_{N}\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f\big|\lesssim\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\big|+\big|p_{t}Sf\big|+|p_{t}|\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|,\qquad\qquad\big|\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\lesssim|p_{t}|\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|.

To prove Proposition
6.4
, we will apply the energy estimate in Proposition
2.16
to
p_{t}Sf
and
|p_{t}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f
, both multiplied by suitable cutoff functions. We begin computing two commutators.

Lemma 6.6
.

We have

\displaystyle\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},S\big]

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{4(r-3M)p_{v}^{2}}{r\Omega^{4}p_{t}}-\frac{2p_{r^{*}}p_{v}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\bigg)\partial_{t}-\frac{3M}{r^{4}}|\not{p}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+2{\mathbb{X}}_{g},

\displaystyle\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}\Omega^{2}}{r^{3}p_{t}}S-\frac{M|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{4}p_{t}}p_{r^{*}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{2}p_{t}}\partial_{t}+\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{r^{2}p_{t}}{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}-\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{p_{t}}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}.

Proof.

We recall from Lemma
5.11
that

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\partial_{r^{*}}]

\displaystyle=\frac{4(r-3M)p_{v}^{2}}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\partial_{t}-2\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}p_{r^{*}}\big(\partial_{t}+\partial_{r^{*}}\big)+\Omega^{2}\frac{r-6M}{r^{5}}|\not{p}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+2\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r}{\mathbb{X}}_{g},

\displaystyle\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{2p_{v}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\partial_{t}-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+\partial_{r^{*}}\big).

As
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(r\Omega^{-2})=p_{r^{*}}\frac{r-4M}{r\Omega^{4}}
, we have

\displaystyle\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},S\big]

\displaystyle=p_{r^{*}}\frac{r-4M}{r\Omega^{4}}\big(\partial_{t}+\partial_{r^{*}}\big)+\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\partial_{r^{*}}]-\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}|\not{p}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}-p_{r^{*}}\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\big],

from where we obtain the first relation. For the second one, we use the null-shell relation (
16
) to show

\displaystyle\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\partial_{t}-\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{\Omega^{2}|p_{t}|^{2}}\partial_{r^{*}}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{2}|p_{t}|^{2}}\partial_{r^{*}}

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\partial_{t}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{2}|p_{t}|^{2}}\partial_{r^{*}}-\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{|p_{t}|^{2}}\bigg({\mathbb{X}}_{g}+\frac{p_{t}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{t}-\frac{1}{r^{2}}{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}-\frac{(r-3M)|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{4}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\bigg).

It remains to use
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(p_{t})=0
and to rewrite
\partial_{r^{*}}
in terms of
S
.
∎

To generate good error terms and absorb the problematic ones, we will multiply the derivatives
Sf
and
p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f
by the weight
\omega(x,p)
defined by

(34)

\omega(x,p)\coloneqq 1-\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{|p_{t}|}\big(3-\log^{-1}(2+r)\big).

We note that
1\leq\omega(x,p)\leq 4
on the domain of incoming particles
\{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0\}
. The error terms arising from the cutoff function will have a good sign as well.

Lemma 6.7
.

For all
r\geq 6M
, we have

|p_{t}|{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\omega)\leq-\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{(2+r)\log^{2}(2+r)}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2r^{3}}.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
be an increasing function such that
\chi(s)=0
for
s\leq 0
, and
\chi(s)=1
for
s\geq 1
. Then,

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big[\chi(r-R)\chi(-p_{r^{*}})\big]\leq 0.

Proof.

As
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(p_{t})=0
, the first inequality follows from
3-\log^{-1}(2+r)\geq 1
,
r-3M\geq\frac{1}{2}r
, and

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big[-p_{r^{*}}\big(3-\log^{-1}(2+r)\big)\big]=-\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{(2+r)\log^{2}(2+r)}-\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}|\not{p}|^{2}\big(3-\log^{-1}(2+r)\big).

For the second one, we have

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big[\chi(r-R)\chi(-p_{r^{*}})\big]=p_{r^{*}}\chi^{\prime}(r-R)\chi(-p_{r^{*}})-\frac{r-3M}{r^{4}}|\not{p}|^{2}\chi^{\prime}(-p_{r^{*}})\chi(r-R).

Finally, it remains to use
\chi^{\prime}\geq 0
as well as
r-3M\geq 0
and
p_{r^{*}}\leq 0
, when the RHS does not vanish.
∎

We are now able to show Proposition
6.4
.

Proof of Proposition
6.4
.

By applying the energy estimate in Proposition
2.16
to the functions

\chi\big[\epsilon^{-1}(r-R)\big]\chi\big(-\epsilon^{-1}p_{r^{*}}\big)\omega|Sf|,\qquad\qquad\chi\big[\epsilon^{-1}(r-R)\big]\chi\big(-\epsilon^{-1}p_{r^{*}}\big)\omega|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f|,

for
\epsilon>0
, we get from the dominated convergence theorem and Lemma
6.7
that

\mathbb{E}\Big[\omega p_{t}|Sf|\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\Big](\tau)\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\omega p_{t}|Sf|\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\Big](0)+\int_{\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R,\;p_{r^{*}}\leq 0\}}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\omega)|p_{t}Sf|+\omega|p_{t}|{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(|Sf|\big){\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\omega p_{t}|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f|\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\Big](\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\omega p_{t}|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f|\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\Big](0)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R,\;p_{r^{*}}\leq 0\}}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\omega)\big|p_{t}^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|+\omega|p_{t}|{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f|\big){\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}.

Applying Lemma
6.6
, we have for
r\geq R
that

\displaystyle|p_{t}|{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(|Sf|\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6p_{t}p_{v}}{\Omega^{4}}|\partial_{t}f|+\frac{3M}{R}\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f|\leq 7|p_{t}||p_{v}||\partial_{t}f|+\frac{3M}{R}\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f|,

and, as
r^{-1}|{\mathbb{X}}_{Q}f|=r^{-1}|\not{p}||{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f|\leq 2|p_{t}||{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f|
for
r\geq R
,

\displaystyle|p_{t}|{\mathbb{X}}\big(|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f|\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}|Sf|+\frac{M}{R}\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f|+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{2}}|\partial_{t}f|+2\frac{|p_{t}|^{2}}{r}\big|{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f\big|.

By the null-shell relation, we have
\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{2}}\leq 6|p_{t}||p_{v}|
. Hence, as
\omega\leq 4
, we get for
r\geq R
,

\displaystyle\omega|p_{t}|{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(|Sf|\big)+\frac{\omega}{16}|p_{t}|{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f|\big)\leq\frac{13M}{R}\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f|+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{4r^{3}}|Sf|+30|p_{t}||p_{v}||\partial_{t}f|+\frac{|p_{t}|^{2}}{2r}\big|{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f\big|.

If
R\geq 2M
is such that
\frac{13M}{R}\leq\frac{1}{64}
, we have from the upper bound for
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\omega)
in Lemma
6.7
that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\omega)|p_{t}Sf|+\omega|p_{t}|{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(|Sf|\big)+\frac{1}{16}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\omega)|p_{t}|\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|+\frac{\omega}{16}|p_{t}|{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f|\big)

(35)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq-\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{(r+2)\log^{2}(2+r)}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{4r^{3}}\bigg)\Big[\big|Sf\big|+\frac{1}{16}\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\Big]+30|p_{t}||p_{v}||\partial_{t}f|+\frac{|p_{t}|^{2}}{2r}\big|{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f\big|.

Proposition
6.4
then follows from the integrated energy estimates satisfied by
\partial_{t}f
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f
. We recall (
33
) and the estimate
|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|\gtrsim r|p_{t}|
in the region being considered.
∎

Remark 6.8
.

We note that the region of outgoing particles near
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, corresponding to
\{r\leq R_{2},\;p_{r^{*}}>0\}
for some
R_{2}\in(2M,2.5M)
, can also be treated independently from the rest of the null-shell. This will however not be required for our purposes.

6.3.
Boundedness and ILED without relative degeneracy for degenerate derivatives

Let us prove the following energy estimates.

Proposition 6.9
.

For all
\tau\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}f\Big](\tau)+\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})\setminus\{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0,\,r\geq R\}}\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}\Big||p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}f\Big|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\lesssim\mathcal{F}[f](0),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\Big](\tau)+\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})\setminus\{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0,\,r\geq R\}}\frac{|p_{t}|}{r\Omega^{2}}\big|\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\lesssim\mathcal{F}[f](0).

Remark 6.10
.

Note that
|p_{t}|\Omega^{-2}\gtrsim|p_{N}|
.

In order to lighten the presentation and until the end of this section
6.3
, we introduce the notation

\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}:=|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}.

For the purpose of controlling
\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f
and
\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f
in
L^{1}(\pi^{-1}(\Sigma_{\tau}))
, we begin computing the following identities.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
f
be a solution to the massless Vlasov equation. Then, there hold

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(r^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f\big)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{-\bigg(\frac{5}{4}\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})-\frac{3{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(|p_{N}|)}{4|p_{N}|}\bigg)r^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f}_{\text{error term with a good sign}}-\underbrace{\frac{(r+3M)|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}}r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f}_{\text{bad error term}},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{-\frac{1}{4}\bigg(\frac{(r^{2}+2Mr+3M^{2})|p_{t}|}{r|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{\frac{3}{2}}+M|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}})}+\frac{2M|p_{v}|}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\bigg)r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f}_{\text{error term with a good sign}}-\underbrace{\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\frac{|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}}{|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}}r^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f}_{\text{bad error term}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{|\Omega^{2}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|p_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{2}\partial_{t}f.

Proof.

Recall from Definition
5.23
that
r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}=|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\Omega^{2}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}
. For the first relation, we write

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}f\big)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}\big)|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}f+{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\big)|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}f+|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\big](f)

and we apply Lemmata
3.8
and
5.21
. For the second one, we have

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big)={\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(|\Omega^{2}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\big)|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f+|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\Omega^{2}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\big](f).

We deal with the last term on the RHS by using the commutation formula in Proposition
5.15
. For the first term, we use Lemma
3.8
and
|p_{u}|-p_{r^{*}}=|p_{v}|
, which provide

(36)

\hskip-11.38109pt{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\Omega^{2}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-})=\Omega^{2}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-})+\frac{2Mp_{r^{*}}}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\Omega^{2}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}=-\frac{(r^{2}+2Mr+3M^{2})|p_{t}|}{r|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{\frac{3}{2}}+M|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}})}\Omega^{2}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}-\frac{2M|p_{v}|}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\Omega^{2}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}.

∎

The next remarks will be crucial for us.

•

The vector fields
r^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}
and
r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}
carry too strong
r
-weights. For this reason, we rescale them by using the well-chosen weight functions
\overline{\omega}_{1/4}
and
\overline{\omega}_{9/4}
which are introduced in Definition
6.12
.

•

The system of commuted equations is not triangular. When performing the energy estimates, we will have to check that the second terms in the relations in Lemma
6.11
can be absorbed by the error terms with a good sign.

•

The good error term in the second equation does not allow to control the bulk integral of
p_{u}\Omega^{-2}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f
near
{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
. We will circumvent this issue by applying Corollary
5.25
, which will not provide new problematic error terms.

•

We will crucially use that we have already controlled the derivatives
\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f
and
\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f
in the region
\{r\geq R,\;p_{r^{*}}<0\}
.

Then, in order to deal with the error terms, we will proceed as follows:

(a)

The bad error term in the first equation can be bounded by a (large) multiple of the good one in the second equation and
|p_{u}\Omega^{-2}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f|
.

(b)

Then, we need to bound
r^{-2}\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|p_{t}|^{3/4}|p_{N}|^{-3/4}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f
by a small multiple of the good error term in the first equation. The strategy is the following.

•

We will generate additional good error terms degenerating at the photon sphere, the future event horizon and spatial infinity. The idea will be to use the weight function
{\zeta}^{A}
, for
A>0
large enough, where
{\zeta}
is introduced in Definition
6.16
and studied in Lemma
6.17
.

•

As
\frac{|p_{t}|}{|p_{N}|}
degenerates near
{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\!
, we will prove
\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})+A|p_{t}|\gg\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|\frac{|p_{t}|^{3/4}}{|p_{N}|^{3/4}}
for
r\sim 2M
.

•

Exploiting that
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}
degenerates at trapping, we will also be able to absorb the error term near
r\sim 3M
by making use of
{\zeta}^{A}
.

•

Finally, since
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\sim rp_{v}
is well behaved for
r\gg 1
and
p_{r^{*}}\geq 0
, we will also be able to deal with a neighborhood of spatial infinity
\{t<+\infty,\,r=+\infty\}
.

We start by defining the weights that allow us to compensate for the
r^{\frac{1}{4}}
-growth of
|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}
and the
r^{\frac{9}{4}}
-growth of
r|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{5}{4}}
.

Definition 6.12
.

Let
\chi\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
be a decreasing function such that
\chi(s)=1
for
s\leq 0
, and
\chi(s)=0
for
s\geq 1
. For
a\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
, we set

\overline{\omega}_{a}(r,p)\coloneqq\frac{1}{M^{a}}\chi(r-R)+\frac{1}{r^{a}}\big[1-\chi(r-R)\big]\chi\Big(2\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{p_{t}}\Big).

Remark 6.13
.

By definition, we have

\displaystyle\forall\,2M\leq r\leq R,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\overline{\omega}_{a}

\displaystyle(r,p)=M^{-a},

\displaystyle\forall\,r\geq R+1,\qquad\qquad\qquad\frac{1}{r^{a}}\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\geq 0}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\,\overline{\omega}_{a}(r,p)\lesssim\frac{1}{r^{a}}\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\geq-\frac{|p_{t}|}{2}}.

Remark 6.14
.

The cutoff function
\chi
will allow, when
p_{r^{*}}\leq 0
and
r\geq R+1
, to use the property that

(37)

|p_{r^{*}}|\leq\frac{1}{2}|p_{t}|\Rightarrow|p_{t}|^{2}\leq\Omega^{2}\frac{4|\not{p}|^{2}}{3r^{2}},

which directly follows from (
16
). This property turns out to be important to deal with terms with borderline spatial decay. We recall from the degenerate ILED of Proposition
4.1
that the
p_{r^{*}}
component requires slightly more spatial decay to be integrable than the spherical ones.

The error terms arising from
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\overline{\omega}_{a})
will be handled as follows.

Lemma 6.15
.

There exists
\overline{C}>0
, depending only on
R
, such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\overline{\omega}_{\frac{1}{4}}\big)\big|r^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f\big|

\displaystyle\leq\overline{C}\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}\bigg)\Big[\big|Sf\big|+\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\Big]\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}+\overline{C}\frac{|p_{t}|}{r}\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\big|,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\overline{\omega}_{\frac{9}{4}}\big)\big|r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|

\displaystyle\leq\overline{C}\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}\bigg)\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}.

Proof.

Let
a\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
. We start by observing, as
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(p_{t})=0
, that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\overline{\omega}_{a})

\displaystyle=p_{r^{*}}\chi^{\prime}(r-R)\Big[\frac{1}{M^{a}}-\frac{1}{r^{a}}\chi\Big(2\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{p_{t}}\Big)\Big]-\frac{ap_{r^{*}}}{r^{a+1}}\big[1-\chi(r-R)\big]\chi\Big(2\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{p_{t}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2(r-3M)|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{a+4}p_{t}}\big[1-\chi(r-R)\big]\chi^{\prime}\Big(2\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{p_{t}}\Big).

By support considerations and since
\chi^{\prime}\leq 0
, we have for
r\geq 2M
that

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\overline{\omega}_{a})\lesssim|p_{r^{*}}|\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\,\mathds{1}_{R\leq r\leq R+1}+\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|}{r^{a+1}}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{a+3}|p_{t}|}\bigg)\mathds{1}_{-\frac{|p_{t}|}{2}\leq p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}.

We now use (
37
) to get

(38)

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\overline{\omega}_{a})\lesssim\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{a+2}}\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\,\mathds{1}_{R\leq r\leq R+1}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{|p_{t}|r^{a+3}}\mathds{1}_{-\frac{|p_{t}|}{2}\leq p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}.

It remains to use Remark
6.5
.
∎

Next, we introduce and study a
W^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
-weight
\zeta
that can be used to derive an ILED that degenerates at
r=2M
, at
r=3M
, and when
r\to+\infty
.

Definition 6.16
.

Let
{\zeta}\colon\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be the weight function

{\zeta}(x,p)\coloneqq 2-M^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{sgn}(p_{r^{*}})\mathrm{sgn}(r-3M)\Big|\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big|\,\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{3M}\Big|^{\frac{1}{4}}.

Lemma 6.17
.

The weight function
{\zeta}
satisfies
1\leq|{\zeta}|\leq 3
. Moreover, we have

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}({\zeta})\lesssim-\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|}{r^{2}}-\bigg|1-\frac{3M}{r}\bigg|\,\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}|p_{t}|}.

Remark 6.18
.

Compared to
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\xi)
, estimated in Proposition
3.4
, the degeneracy at the photon sphere is linear in
|p_{r^{*}}|+|r-3M|
and not quadratic. This will allow for simplifications in the upcoming analysis.

Proof.

The first statement ensues from
r\geq 2M
and
|p_{r^{*}}|\leq|p_{t}|
. For the second one, as
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(p_{t})=0
, we have

\displaystyle M^{-\frac{1}{4}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}({\zeta})

\displaystyle=-\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{\frac{5}{4}}}{4r^{2}}\Big|\,\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{3M}\Big|^{-\frac{3}{4}}-\frac{\mathrm{sgn}(r-3M)}{4|p_{r^{*}}|^{\frac{3}{4}}}\Big|\,\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{3M}\Big|^{\frac{1}{4}}\cdot\frac{(r-3M)|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim-\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{\frac{5}{4}}}{r^{\frac{5}{4}}|r-3M|^{\frac{3}{4}}}-|r-3M|^{\frac{5}{4}}\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{\frac{17}{4}}|p_{r^{*}}|^{\frac{3}{4}}}.

Note that, as
|p_{r^{*}}|\leq|p_{t}|
, it implies the stated estimate for the region
\{r\leq 2.5M\}\cup\{r\geq 4M\}
. Next, we note that if
2.5\leq r\leq 4M
, then
|p_{t}|\sim|p_{r^{*}}|+|\not{p}|
and
1\lesssim|r-3M|^{-1}
. Consequently, we have on this region

|p_{r^{*}}|\leq\frac{4}{5}\cdot\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{\frac{5}{4}}}{|r-3M|^{\frac{1}{4}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\frac{1}{5}\cdot|r-3M||p_{t}|\lesssim\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{\frac{5}{4}}}{|r-3M|^{\frac{3}{4}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}}+|r-3M|\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{|p_{t}|}

as well as

|r-3M|\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{|p_{t}|}\leq\frac{4}{5}\cdot\frac{|r-3M|^{\frac{5}{4}}|\not{p}|^{\frac{5}{2}}}{|p_{r^{*}}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{1}{5}\cdot\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{3}}{|p_{t}|^{2}}\lesssim|r-3M|^{\frac{5}{4}}\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{|p_{r^{*}}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{\frac{5}{4}}}{|r-3M|^{\frac{3}{4}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}}.

These last two inequalities allow to conclude the proof.
∎

We now start the proof of Proposition
6.9
. Let us consider a constant
A>0
that will be chosen sufficiently large. We apply the energy estimate of Proposition
2.16
in order to have

\mathbb{E}\Big[\overline{\omega}_{\frac{1}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}r^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f\Big](\tau)\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\overline{\omega}_{\frac{1}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}r^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f\Big](0)+\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\tau})}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\overline{\omega}_{\frac{1}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f\big|\Big){\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\overline{\omega}_{\frac{9}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\Big](\tau)\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\overline{\omega}_{\frac{9}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\Big](0)+\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{\tau})}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\overline{\omega}_{\frac{9}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\Big){\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}.

We now make two observations:

•

In order to prove Proposition
6.9
, it is enough to control
\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f
and
\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f
in the set
\{r\leq R\}\cup\{r\geq R,\;p_{r^{*}}\geq 0\}
, in view of Proposition
6.4
and Remark
6.5
.

•

On the region
\{r\leq R\}\cup\{r\geq R,\;p_{r^{*}}\geq 0\}
, we have
\overline{\omega}_{1/4}\sim r^{-1/4}
,
\overline{\omega}_{9/4}\sim r^{-9/4}
and
{\zeta}\sim 1
.

Thus, in view of the ILED (
33
) satisfied by
\partial_{t}f
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f
and Proposition
6.4
, a sufficient condition for Proposition
6.9
to hold is given in the following lemma.

Lemma 6.19
.

For
A>0
large enough, there exists
c>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\overline{\omega}_{\frac{1}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f\big|\Big)+{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\overline{\omega}_{\frac{9}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\Big)+c\bigg(\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}\big|\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f\big|+\frac{|p_{t}|}{r\Omega^{2}}\big|\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\{p_{r^{*}}<0,\,r\geq R\}^{\mathsf{c}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\big|+\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f\big|+\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}\bigg)\Big[\big|Sf\big|+\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\Big]\,\mathds{1}_{\{p_{r^{*}}<0,\,r\geq R\}}.

Proof.

Recall first
|p_{t}|\leq|p_{N}|
and Proposition
3.5
, which in particular implies

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(|p_{N}|)\lesssim|p_{t}|^{2}\,\mathds{1}_{2.5M\leq r\leq 2.7M}\lesssim\Big|1-\frac{3M}{r}\Big||p_{t}|^{2}\mathds{1}_{r\leq R}.

Next, we have from Lemma
6.17
that
{\zeta}^{A}\leq 3^{A}
and

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}({\zeta}^{A})=A{\mathbb{X}}_{g}({\zeta}){\zeta}^{A-1}\leq A{\mathbb{X}}_{g}({\zeta}){\zeta}^{A}\lesssim-A\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|}{r^{2}}+\bigg|1-\frac{3M}{r}\bigg|\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}|p_{t}|}\bigg){\zeta}^{A}.

We further stress that
|p_{t}|\leq|p_{r^{*}}|+|\not{p}|r^{-1}
. Thus, according to Lemmata
6.11
and
6.15
, there exists
b>0
depending only on
M
, such that if
A
is large enough,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\overline{\omega}_{\frac{1}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f\big|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq-\mathfrak{F}(r,p)\overline{\omega}_{\frac{1}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f\big|+\frac{(r+3M)|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}}\overline{\omega}_{\frac{1}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|

\displaystyle\quad+3^{A}\overline{C}\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}\bigg)\Big(\big|Sf\big|+\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\Big)\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}+3^{A}\overline{C}\frac{|p_{t}|}{r}\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\big|,

where the function
\mathfrak{F}(r,p)
is defined as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}(r,p)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{5(r^{2}+2Mr+3M^{2})|p_{t}|}{4r|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{\frac{3}{2}}+M|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}})}+\frac{5M|p_{u}|}{2r^{2}\Omega^{2}}+bA\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|}{r^{2}}+\bigg|1-\frac{3M}{r}\bigg|\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}|p_{t}|}\bigg).

Similarly, there exists
\widetilde{C}>0
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\overline{\omega}_{\frac{9}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\Big)
