\displaystyle\leq-\mathfrak{G}_{0}(r,p)\overline{\omega}_{\frac{9}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|+\frac{\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}}{|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}}\overline{\omega}_{\frac{9}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f\big|

\displaystyle\quad+3^{A}\overline{C}\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}\bigg)|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f|\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}+3^{A}\widetilde{C}\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}\Big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\Big|,

with

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{0}(r,p)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{(r^{2}+2Mr+3M^{2})|p_{t}|}{4r|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{\frac{3}{2}}+M|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}})}+\frac{M|p_{v}|}{2r^{2}\Omega^{2}}+bA\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|}{r^{2}}+\bigg|1-\frac{3M}{r}\bigg|\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}|p_{t}|}\bigg)

\displaystyle\,\geq\frac{|p_{t}|}{4r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{M|p_{v}|}{2r^{2}\Omega^{2}}+bA\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|}{r^{2}}+\bigg|1-\frac{3M}{r}\bigg|\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}|p_{t}|}\bigg),

and where we controlled the term proportional to
\partial_{t}f
by using

|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|\lesssim r|p_{N}|,\qquad|\Omega^{2}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|\lesssim r|p_{t}|,\qquad\overline{\omega}_{\frac{9}{4}}\lesssim r^{-\frac{9}{4}},\qquad{\zeta}^{A}\leq 3^{A}.

Note that
\mathfrak{G}_{0}(r,p)
does not control
|p_{u}|\Omega^{-2}
near
{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
. To deal with this issue, we apply Corollary
5.25
. It implies that there exists
C_{0}>0
, depending only on
M
, such that, if
A
is large enough,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\overline{\omega}_{\frac{9}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\Big)\leq-\mathfrak{G}(r,p)\overline{\omega}_{\frac{9}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|+\big(1+C_{0}\mathds{1}_{r\leq 2.7M}\big)\frac{\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}}{|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}}\overline{\omega}_{\frac{9}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f\big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\!3^{A}\overline{C}\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}\!|^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}\bigg)|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f|\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0}\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}+3^{A}\widetilde{C}\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}\Big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\Big|+3^{A}C_{0}\mathds{1}_{r\leq 2.7M}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}p_{N}|\big(|\partial_{t}f|+|{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f|\big),

where

(39)

\mathfrak{G}(r,p)\coloneqq\frac{|p_{t}|}{4r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{M|p_{v}|}{2r^{2}\Omega^{2}}+\frac{M|p_{u}|}{2r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\mathds{1}_{r\leq 2.7M}.

We recall Remark
6.5
and the properties of
\overline{\omega}_{a}
, in particular (
37
). Then, we have that in the domain
\{r\geq R,\;p_{r^{*}}<0\}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\overline{\omega}_{\frac{1}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}}f\big|\Big)+{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\overline{\omega}_{\frac{9}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}\big|r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f\big|\Big)\lesssim\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}}\bigg)\big(|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f|+|Sf|\big)+\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\big|.

Finally, the result is a consequence of the following technical Lemma
6.20
.
∎

Lemma 6.20
.

We have, on
\{r\leq R\}\cup\{r\geq R,\,p_{r^{*}}\geq 0\}
, that

\mathfrak{G}(r,p)-\frac{1}{8}\cdot\frac{(r+3M)|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\overline{\omega}_{1/4}}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\omega}_{9/4}}\gtrsim\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}.

and, if
A
is large enough,

\frac{1}{8}\mathfrak{F}(r,p)-\frac{\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}||p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}\overline{\omega}_{9/4}}{|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}\overline{\omega}_{1/4}}\big(1+C_{0}\mathds{1}_{r\leq 2.7M}\big)\gtrsim\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}

Proof.

We will prove several estimates which, together with
\mathfrak{F}(r,p),\,\mathfrak{G}(r,p)\gtrsim|p_{N}|r^{-1}
, will imply the result. We focus first on the domain
\{r\geq R+1,\,p_{r^{*}}\geq 0\}
, with
R\geq 832M
. For this, we will use the next properties.

•

For
r\geq R+1
, we have
p_{N}=p_{t}
,
\overline{\omega}_{1/4}(r,p)=r^{-1/4}
and
\overline{\omega}_{9/4}(r,p)=r^{-9/4}
.

•

If
p_{r^{*}}\geq 0
, then
2|p_{u}|=|p_{t}|+p_{r^{*}}\geq|p_{t}|
.

•

For
r\geq 3M
, we have by Remark
3.7
that

\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}(x,p)=\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}(p_{t}+p_{r^{*}})-\frac{27M^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-3M)+r^{\frac{3}{2}}}p_{t}.

Consequently, in view of the null-shell relation
4r^{2}p_{u}p_{v}=\Omega^{2}|\not{p}|^{2}
, one obtains that for
r\geq R+1
and
p_{r^{*}}\geq 0
,

\frac{\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}||p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}\overline{\omega}_{9/4}}{|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}\overline{\omega}_{1/4}}=\frac{\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{r^{2}}\leq\frac{2|p_{v}|}{r\Omega^{2}}+\frac{27M^{2}|p_{t}|}{r^{3}}\leq\frac{4|p_{v}||p_{u}|}{r\Omega^{2}|p_{t}|}+\frac{27M^{2}|p_{t}|}{r^{3}}\leq 2\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}|p_{t}|}+\frac{27M^{2}|p_{r^{*}}|}{r^{3}}.

Hence, if
A
is large enough, we have

\frac{\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}||p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}\overline{\omega}_{9/4}}{|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}\overline{\omega}_{1/4}}\leq\frac{bA}{16}\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|}{r^{2}}+\bigg|1-\frac{3M}{r}\bigg|\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}|p_{t}|}\bigg)\leq\frac{1}{16}\mathfrak{F}(r,p).

For the other inequality, note that

\frac{(r+3M)|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\overline{\omega}_{1/4}}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\omega}_{9/4}}=\frac{(r+3M)|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}(R)}\leq 5\mathfrak{G}(r,p).

Assume now that
2.7M\leq r\leq R+1
, so that
p_{N}=p_{t}
and
\overline{\omega}_{1/4}/\overline{\omega}_{9/4}\leq 2M^{2}
. Hence,

\frac{(r+3M)|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\overline{\omega}_{1/4}}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\omega}_{9/4}}\leq\frac{2M^{2}|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq 4\mathfrak{G}(r,p).

Since
\Omega^{2}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|\leq r|p_{r^{*}}|+2|r-3M||p_{t}|
, we have for a certain constant
C>0
depending only on
M
,

\frac{\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}||p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}\overline{\omega}_{9/4}}{|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}\overline{\omega}_{1/4}}\leq C(|p_{r^{*}}|+|r-3M||p_{t}|)\leq\frac{bA}{16}\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|}{r^{2}}+\bigg|1-\frac{3M}{r}\bigg|\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}|p_{t}|}\bigg)\leq\frac{1}{16}\mathfrak{F}(r,p),

provided that
A
is large enough.

We finally consider the case
r\leq 2.7M
, so that
\overline{\omega}_{a}=M^{-a}
. As
|p_{N}|\leq 16|p_{t}|\Omega^{-2}
, we have

\frac{(r+3M)|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\overline{\omega}_{1/4}}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\omega}_{9/4}}\leq\frac{8M^{2}|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\leq\frac{2M|p_{t}|}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}=2M\frac{|p_{v}|+|p_{u}|}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\leq 4\mathfrak{G}(r,p).

We conclude the proof by using
|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|\lesssim|p_{N}|
and
\Omega^{2}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|\lesssim|p_{t}|
, which implies

\frac{\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}||p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}\overline{\omega}_{9/4}}{|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}\overline{\omega}_{1/4}}\leq C|p_{t}|\leq\frac{bA}{16}\bigg(\frac{|p_{r^{*}}|}{r^{2}}+\bigg|1-\frac{3M}{r}\bigg|\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}|p_{t}|}\bigg)\leq\frac{1}{16}\mathfrak{F}(r,p),

for
A
chosen large enough.
∎

6.4.
Boundedness and ILED without relative degeneracy for
p_{N}\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}

We are now able to control the derivative
\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f
.

Proposition 6.21
.

For all
\tau\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[p_{N}\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f\Big](\tau)+\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})}\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}\big|p_{N}\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f\big|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\lesssim\mathcal{F}[f](0).

Proof.

Recall the weight function
\xi(x,p)
introduced in Section
3.2
, which verifies
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(|\xi|)\leq 0
and
|\xi|\sim|p_{N}|
. By Corollary
5.22
, we have

(40)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\big|\xi\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f\big|\big)+\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\big|\xi\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f\big|\lesssim\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\big|+\frac{|p_{N}|}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}}|p_{t}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}f|.

Then, we apply the energy estimate of Proposition
2.16
to
|\xi\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f|
and we control the integral over
\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})
of the two error terms in the RHS of (
40
) as follows.

•

We use (
33
) in order to deal with the first one.

•

For the second one, we apply Proposition
6.9
for the region
\{r\leq R\}\cup\{p_{r^{*}}\geq 0,\,r\geq R\}
. For the domain
\{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0,\,r\geq R\}
, we use Remark
6.5
, Proposition
6.4
and (
33
).

∎

We then obtain Proposition
6.3
from (
33
), Proposition
6.4
, Remark
6.5
as well as Propositions
6.9
and
6.21
. Note that we also used
|p_{n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}}|\lesssim|p_{N}|
and (
coarea
).

In the perspective of applying the
r^{p}
-weighted energy method through Proposition
4.6
, we conclude this subsection with the following result. Let us define

\displaystyle g_{1}\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f,\qquad\quad g_{2}\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f,\qquad\quad g_{3}\coloneqq p_{t}Sf\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0},\qquad\quad g_{4}\coloneqq|p_{t}|^{2}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}f\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq R}\,\mathds{1}_{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad g_{5}\coloneqq\overline{\omega}_{\frac{1}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}f,\qquad\quad g_{6}\coloneqq\overline{\omega}_{\frac{9}{4}}{\zeta}^{A}r^{\frac{9}{4}}\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}f,\qquad\quad g_{7}\coloneqq\xi\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f,

with
A>0
chosen large enough in accordance with Lemmata
6.19
–
6.20
.

Proposition 6.22
.

Let
k\in\llbracket 1,7\rrbracket
. There exist constants
c_{k}>0
such that

(41)

\sum_{1\leq k\leq 7}c_{k}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(|g_{k}|)\lesssim-\sum_{1\leq k\leq 7}\frac{|p_{N}|}{r\log^{2}(2+r)}|g_{k}|.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
3.8
, the identities
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\partial_{t}f)={\mathbb{X}}_{g}({\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}f)=0
, the estimate (
35
), Lemma
6.19
and (
40
).
∎

6.5.
Proof of Proposition
6.2

Our argument will rely on a Sobolev inequality involving the vector field
G
defined in (
4
). We introduce first a coordinate system on Schwarzschild spacetime that is related to the integral curves of
r^{-1}\Omega^{-1}G
. These regular hyperboloidal coordinates have been previously considered in
[
Mav23
]
.

Definition 6.23
.

The hyperboloidal coordinate system
(\overline{t},r,\theta,\phi)\in{\mathbb{R}}\times(2M,+\infty)\times(0,\pi)\times(0,2\pi)
is defined by

\overline{t}\coloneqq t-H(r),\qquad H(r)\coloneqq\int_{3M}^{r}\frac{\xi(s)}{\Omega^{2}(s)}{\mathrm{d}}s,\qquad\xi(r)\coloneqq\Big(1-\frac{3M}{r}\Big)\Big(1+\frac{6M}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

To avoid any confusion, we denote by
\overline{\partial}_{x^{\mu}}
the derivatives with respect to the variables
x^{\mu}
in the hyperboloidal coordinate system.

We recall
t^{*}=t+2M\log(r-2M)
and we collect several useful properties.

Proposition 6.24
.

The following properties hold:

(a)

The Jacobian determinant of the map
(t,r,\theta,\phi)\mapsto(\overline{t},r,\theta,\phi)
is constant equal to one.

(b)

We have the relations

\overline{\partial}_{r}=\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\Big(1-\frac{3M}{r}\Big)\Big(1+\frac{6M}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t},\qquad\overline{\partial}_{t}=\partial_{t},\qquad\overline{\partial}_{\theta}=\partial_{\theta},\qquad\overline{\partial}_{\phi}=\partial_{\phi}.

(c)

The vector fields
G
and
\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}
are related to the vector field
\overline{\partial}_{r}
through the relations

\Omega^{-1}G={\mathrm{d}}\pi\big(\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}\big)=r\overline{\partial}_{r}.

(d)

If
\epsilon>0
is small enough, then we have the following inclusions

\Big\{t^{*}\geq 2,\;|r-3M|\leq\epsilon\Big\}\subset\Big\{\overline{t}+2M\log(M)\geq 1,\;|r-3M|\leq\epsilon\Big\}\subset\Big\{t^{*}\geq 0,\;|r-3M|\leq\epsilon\Big\}.

Moreover, we set

\overline{g}\big(\overline{t},r,\theta,\phi,p\big)\coloneqq g\big(\overline{t}+H(r),r^{*}(r),\theta,\phi,p\big).

We are now able perform the proof of Proposition
6.2
. We then assume that
\mathcal{E}[f](0)<+\infty
.

Proof of Proposition
6.2
.

We first recall the volume form
{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}=h(r){\mathrm{d}}\tau{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\Sigma_{\tau}}{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}
, where
h(r)\sim 1
. For convenience, we introduce for a distribution function
g
the quantities

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}[g]

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})\,\cap\,\{\overline{t}+2M\log(M)\geq 1,\;|r-3M|\leq\epsilon\}}|\not{p}||p_{t}||g|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}},

\displaystyle I[g](r)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\overline{t}=1+2M\log(M)}^{+\infty}\int_{\theta=0}^{\pi}\int_{\phi=0}^{2\pi}\int_{p_{r^{*}}\in{\mathbb{R}}}\int_{p_{\theta}\in{\mathbb{R}}}\int_{p_{\phi}\in{\mathbb{R}}}|\not{p}||\overline{g}|{\mathrm{d}}p_{\phi}{\mathrm{d}}p_{\theta}{\mathrm{d}}p_{r^{*}}{\mathrm{d}}\phi{\mathrm{d}}\theta{\mathrm{d}}\overline{t},

where
\epsilon<M
is a constant that will be fixed small enough. In view of the expression of the volume form
{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}
, we have

(42)

\mathcal{J}[g]\sim\int_{|r-3M|\leq\epsilon}I[g](r){\mathrm{d}}r.

We also note that

\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})\,\cap\,\{0\leq t^{*}\leq 2,\;|r-3M|\leq\epsilon\}}|\not{p}||p_{t}||g|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\lesssim\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq 2}\mathbb{E}\big[p_{N}g\big](\tau)\leq\mathcal{E}[g](0).

Let us come back to the study of the solution
f
of the massless Vlasov equation. In view of
|\not{p}|^{2}\lesssim|\not{p}||p_{t}|
for
r\sim 3M
, the ILED in Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
6.3
, it suffices to show

(43)

\mathcal{J}[f]\lesssim\mathcal{E}[f](0)

in order to prove Proposition
6.2
. According to (
42
), we have

(44)

\mathcal{J}[f]\lesssim\epsilon\sup_{|r-3M|\leq\epsilon}I[f]\big(r\big).

The factor
\epsilon>0
will allow us to absorb in the LHS terms proportional to
\mathcal{J}[f]
that we cannot control a priori by
\mathcal{E}[f](0)
. We now apply a local Sobolev inequality to have

\sup_{|r-3M|\leq\epsilon}I[f]\big(r\big)\lesssim\int_{|r-3M|\leq\epsilon}I[f]+\big|\overline{\partial}_{r}I[f]\big|{\mathrm{d}}r.

To deal with the radial derivatives, we stress that
|\not{p}|
is independent of
(t,r,p_{r^{*}})
and we write

r\overline{\partial}_{r}\big|\overline{f}\big|=\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}(|f|)+\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r\Omega^{2}}p_{r^{*}}+\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}p_{t}\bigg)\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}|f|-\Phi r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}|f|.

Consequently, as
\Omega^{-1}
and
r
are bounded on
\{|r-3M|\leq\epsilon\}
, we obtain by integration by parts in
p_{r^{*}}
that

\sup_{|r-3M|\leq\epsilon}I[f](r)\lesssim\int_{|r-3M|\leq\epsilon}I\big[\,\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f\big]+I\big[(1+|\Phi|+|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Phi|)f\big]{\mathrm{d}}r.

We recall now that
\Phi
and
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Phi
are bounded in
L_{x,p}^{\infty}(\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R}))
from Proposition
5.20
. Thus, by (
42
) and the ILED of Proposition
6.3
, we have

\sup_{|r-3M|\leq\epsilon}I[f](r)\lesssim\int_{|r-3M|\leq\epsilon}I\big[\,\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f\big]+I\big[f\big]{\mathrm{d}}r\lesssim\mathcal{J}\big[\,\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f\big]+\mathcal{J}[f]\lesssim\mathcal{F}[f](0)+\mathcal{J}[f].

We then deduce (
43
) from this last estimate and (
44
), by choosing
\epsilon
small enough.
∎

7.
Decay of the energy flux

Let us now show quantitative decay estimates for the first order energy flux
\mathcal{E}[f]
studied in the previous section. For this, we use the
r^{p}
-weighted energy method suitably adapted to the framework in place.

7.1.
The
r^{p}
-weighted energy method

Let us begin by proving the following elementary result.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}.
For
q\in\llbracket 0,p\rrbracket
, let
\mathcal{F}_{q}\colon{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
be functions satisfying the following properties:

(a)

Uniform boundedness. There exist constants
\overline{C}_{q}>0
such that

\forall\,\tau_{2}\geq\tau_{1}\geq 0,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{F}_{q}(\tau_{2})\leq\overline{C}_{q}\mathcal{F}_{q}(\tau_{1}).

(b)

Hierarchy of integrated energy decay estimates. For any
q\in\llbracket 1,p\rrbracket
, we have

\forall\,\tau_{1}\geq\tau_{2}\geq 0,\qquad\qquad\int_{\tau=\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\mathcal{F}_{q-1}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq\overline{C}_{q}\mathcal{F}_{q}(\tau_{1}).

Then, there exists a constant
C_{p}>0
such that

\forall\,\tau\geq 0,\quad\qquad\mathcal{F}_{0}(\tau)\leq\frac{C_{p}}{\langle\tau\rangle^{p}}\mathcal{F}_{p}(0).

Proof.

We note that the result holds for
0\leq\tau\leq 1
in view of the assumption
(a)
. We deal with the case
\tau\geq 1
by performing an induction. Let us prove that there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(IH-q)

\forall\,\tau\geq 1,\quad\qquad\mathcal{F}_{q}(\tau)\leq\frac{C}{\tau^{p-q}}\mathcal{F}_{p}(0)

holds for any
q\in\llbracket 0,p\rrbracket
. By
(a)
, the induction hypothesis holds for
q=p
. Let
q\in\llbracket 1,p\rrbracket
such that (
IH-q
) holds. Fix
i\in\mathbb{N}
and use the first assumption
(a)
for
(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=(\tau,2^{i+1})
to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\tau=2^{i}}^{2^{i+1}}\mathcal{F}_{q-1}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\geq\int_{\tau=2^{i}}^{2^{i+1}}\frac{1}{\overline{C}_{q-1}}\mathcal{F}_{q-1}(2^{i+1}){\mathrm{d}}\tau=\frac{2^{i}}{\overline{C}_{q-1}}\mathcal{F}_{q-1}(2^{i+1}).

Combining this estimate with the assumption
(b)
applied on the interval
(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=(2^{i},2^{i+1})
, we obtain from the induction hypothesis that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{q-1}(2^{i+1})\leq\frac{\overline{C}_{q-1}}{2^{i}}\int_{\tau=2^{i}}^{2^{i+1}}\mathcal{F}_{q-1}(\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq\frac{\overline{C}_{q-1}}{2^{i}}\cdot\overline{C}_{q}\mathcal{F}_{q}(2^{i})\leq\frac{\overline{C}_{q-1}\overline{C}_{q}}{2^{i}}\cdot\frac{C}{2^{(p-q)i}}\mathcal{F}_{p}(0).

Let us consider
\tau\geq 2
since the case
\tau\leq 2
is straightforward by
(a)
. Then, there exists
i\in\mathbb{N}
such that
2^{i+1}\leq\tau<2^{i+2}
, so
2^{i}\sim\tau
. Using again the assumption
(a)
, we have

\mathcal{F}_{q-1}(\tau)\leq\overline{C}_{q-1}\mathcal{F}_{q-1}(2^{i+1})\leq|\overline{C}_{q-1}|^{2}\overline{C}_{q}\cdot\frac{C}{2^{(p-q+1)i}}\mathcal{F}_{p}(0)\lesssim\frac{1}{\tau^{p-q+1}}\mathcal{F}_{p}(0).

By considering a larger constant
C
, the induction hypothesis holds at step
q-1
. From here the proof follows.
∎

7.2.
Decay of the energy flux
\mathcal{E}[f]

For
p\geq 0
and
g\colon\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
, we define the norm

\mathbb{E}^{p}[g]\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\langle r^{p}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p}{2}\rceil}\Big\rangle\,g\Big].

Remark 7.2
.

As a direct application of Lemma
7.1
, we get from Proposition
4.4
applied to
\partial_{t}f
that

\forall\,\tau\geq 0,\qquad\quad\mathbb{E}\big[\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\big](\tau)\lesssim_{p}\langle\tau\rangle^{-p}\,\mathbb{E}^{p}\big[\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}f\big](0).

We set further the following energy fluxes,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{p}[g]

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbb{E}^{p}\big[\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{t}g\big]+\mathbb{E}^{p}\big[\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}g\big]+\mathbb{E}^{p}\big[p_{N}\,\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}g\big]+\mathbb{E}^{p}\big[|p_{N}|^{\frac{3}{4}}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\frac{1}{4}}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}g\big]+\mathbb{E}^{p}\big[\mathrm{D}_{p_{r^{*}}}g\big],

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{p}[g]

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{F}^{p}[g]+\mathbb{E}^{p}[p_{N}g].

We now prove the main result of this section.

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
p_{0}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
f
be a solution to
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(f)=0
. For any
p\in\llbracket 1,p_{0}\rrbracket
, we have

(45)

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\,\mathcal{E}^{p}[f](\tau)+\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\mathcal{E}^{p-1}[f](\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{p}[f](0).

Moreover, there holds

(46)

\forall\,\tau\geq 0,\qquad\mathcal{E}[f](\tau)\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{E}^{p_{0}}[f](0)}{\langle\tau\rangle^{p_{0}}}.

Proof.

Let us observe first that (
46
) is a consequence of (
45
) and Lemma
7.1
. Since the exterior of Schwarzschild black hole is static or, alternatively, by applying (
45
) for the foliation
(\Sigma^{\prime}_{\tau})_{\tau\geq 0}\coloneqq(\Sigma_{\tau+\tau_{1}})_{\tau\geq 0}
, we get

\forall\,\tau_{1}\geq 0,\qquad\sup_{\tau\geq\tau_{1}}\,\mathcal{E}^{p}[f](\tau)+\int_{\tau=\tau_{1}}^{+\infty}\mathcal{E}^{p-1}[f](\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{p}[f](\tau_{1}).

The stated decay rate of the energy flux then follows from
\mathcal{E}[f]=\mathcal{E}^{0}[f]
and Lemma
7.1
.
According to Proposition
6.22
and Proposition
4.6
, for any
p\in\llbracket 1,p_{0}\rrbracket
, we have

(47)

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\,\mathcal{F}^{p}[f](\tau)+\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\mathcal{F}^{p-1}[f](\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim\mathcal{F}^{p}[f](0).

Next, by Corollary
3.15
, where the cutoff function
\overline{\chi}
is introduced, and
{\mathbb{X}}(p_{t}f)=0
, we have

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\overline{\chi}(r)\frac{r^{p}}{\Omega^{2p}}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p}{2}\rceil}|p_{t}f|\Big)\lesssim|p_{t}|^{2}|f|\mathds{1}_{4M\leq r\leq 7M}-r^{p-1}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p-1}{2}\rceil}|p_{v}||p_{t}f|\mathds{1}_{r\geq 7M}.

Hence, by the energy estimate of Proposition
2.16
and since
2\Omega^{2}\geq 1
for
r\geq 4M
, we have, for all
\tau\geq 0
,

(Eq-p)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[r^{p}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p}{2}\rceil}p_{t}f\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq 7M}\bigg](\tau)+\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})}\!r^{p-1}

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p-1}{2}\rceil}|p_{v}||p_{t}f|\mathds{1}_{r\geq 7M}{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\lesssim\mathbb{E}\bigg[r^{p}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\lceil\frac{p}{2}\rceil}p_{t}f\,\mathds{1}_{r\geq 4M}\bigg](0)+\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})}\!\frac{|p_{t}|^{2}}{r^{2}}|f|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}.

Recall now that
p_{n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}}=p_{v}
for
r>R_{0}
. By adding the estimate of Proposition
6.2
multiplied by a sufficiently large constant to (
Eq-p
) and (Eq-1), we have

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\,\mathbb{E}^{p}[p_{N}f](\tau)+\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\mathbb{E}^{p-1}[p_{N}f](\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim\mathbb{E}^{p}[p_{N}f](0)+\mathcal{E}[f](0).

The result follows by (
47
).
∎

Remark 7.4
.

The estimate (
46
) can be extended to the case
p_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
instead of
p_{0}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
(see Remark
4.5
).

Finally, we prove that if the initial data is exponentially decaying, then one can prove an integrated energy decay estimate that leads to exponential decay for the energy flux. For
b>0
, we introduce

\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{exp}}^{b}[g]\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{br\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}}\,g\Big]

and we define accordingly
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{exp}}^{b}[g]
as well as
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{exp}}^{b}[g]
.

Proposition 7.5
.

There exists a constant
0<b_{0}\leq b
, depending on
M
and
b
, such that

\forall\,\tau\geq 0,\qquad\mathcal{E}[f](\tau)\lesssim e^{-b_{0}\tau}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{exp}}^{b}[f](0).

Proof.

Note first, in view of the support of the cutoff function
\overline{\chi}
,

e^{b\,\overline{\chi}(r)\frac{rp_{v}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}}\sim e^{b\frac{rp_{v}}{p_{t}}}\quad\text{for $r\leq 2M$,}\qquad\qquad\qquad e^{b\frac{rp_{v}}{p_{t}}}\sim 1\quad\text{for $r\leq\max(7M,R_{0})$}.

Hence, according to Corollary
3.15
, applied for
p=1
, we have

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(e^{b\,\overline{\chi}(r)\frac{rp_{v}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}}\Big)=b{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\overline{\chi}(r)\frac{rp_{v}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\Big)e^{b\,\overline{\chi}(r)\frac{rp_{v}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}}\lesssim|p_{N}|\mathds{1}_{r\leq 7M}-|p_{v}|e^{b\frac{rp_{v}}{p_{t}}}\mathds{1}_{r\geq 7M}.

We then get from Proposition
6.22
that there exists a sufficiently large constant
A>0
such that

(48)

\sum_{1\leq k\leq 7}c_{k}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big[\Big(A+e^{b\,\overline{\chi}(r)\frac{rp_{v}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}}\Big)|g_{k}|\Big]\lesssim-\Big(|p_{N}|\mathds{1}_{r\leq R_{0}}+|p_{v}|e^{b\frac{rp_{v}}{p_{t}}}\mathds{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\Big)\sum_{1\leq k\leq 7}|g_{k}|.

Recall that
p_{n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}}\sim p_{N}
for
r\leq R_{0}
and
p_{n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}}=p_{v}
for
r>R_{0}
. By the energy estimate of Proposition
2.16
, we then obtain

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\,\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{exp}}^{b}[f](\tau)+\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})}|p_{n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}}|e^{b\frac{rp_{v}}{p_{t}}}\sum_{1\leq k\leq 7}|g_{k}|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\lesssim\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{exp}}^{b}[f](0).

Similarly, one has

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\,\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{exp}}^{b}[p_{t}f](\tau)+\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})}|p_{n_{\Sigma_{\tau}}}|e^{b\frac{rp_{v}}{p_{t}}}|p_{t}||f|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{exp}}^{b}[p_{t}f](0)+\int_{\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})\cap\{r\leq R_{0}\}}|p_{N}||p_{t}||f|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}}.

The last term on the RHS is bounded by
\mathcal{E}[f](0)\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{exp}}^{b}[f](0)
according to Proposition
6.2
. This yields,

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{exp}}^{b}[f](\tau)+\int_{\tau=0}^{+\infty}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{exp}}^{b}[f](\tau){\mathrm{d}}\tau\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{exp}}^{b}[f](0).

Using again the time-invariance of the exterior of Schwarzschild black hole, we get that the same estimate holds but with initial time
\tau_{1}
instead of
0
, for all
\tau_{1}\geq 0
. This directly implies the exponential decay.
∎

8.
Pointwise decay estimates

We start by collecting some relations which will be useful later.

Lemma 8.1
.

There holds

\displaystyle\bigg|\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}(p_{t})+\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}}p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim|p_{t}|\qquad

on
\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})
,

\displaystyle\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}(p_{N})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim|p_{N}|\qquad

on
\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R})
,

\displaystyle\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}(p_{v})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim|p_{v}|\qquad

\displaystyle\text{on $\{r\geq R_{0}\}$}.

Moreover, for any complete lift
\widehat{K}\in\{\partial_{t},\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{i},\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{2},\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{3}\}
, we have

\widehat{K}(r)=\widehat{K}(p_{r^{*}})=\widehat{K}(|\not{p}|)=\widehat{K}(p_{t})=\widehat{K}(p_{N})=0.

Remark 8.2
.

Note that the first relation implies
|\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}(p_{t})|\lesssim|p_{N}|
.

Proof.

We start with the first inequality. Since

r\partial_{r}(p_{t})=\frac{r}{2}\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{2}}\bigg)\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{p_{t}}=-\frac{(r-3M)|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}p_{t}},

we have

(49)

\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}(p_{t})=-\frac{(r-3M)|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{3}p_{t}}-\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}+\frac{27M^{2}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}p_{t}\bigg)\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{p_{t}}+\Phi r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{p_{t}}.

Since
r|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\Phi|\lesssim|p_{t}|
by Proposition
5.20
, the last term is bounded above by
|p_{t}|
. The same holds true for the third one. Finally, we compute

p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}-\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{p_{t}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}=r\frac{|p_{t}|^{2}-|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}=\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{rp_{t}}.

Note now that

\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}\big(p_{t}\pm p_{r^{*}}\big)=-\frac{4(r-3M)p_{u}p_{v}}{r\Omega^{2}p_{t}}-\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}+\frac{27M^{2}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{3}{2}}}p_{t}-\Phi r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\bigg)\frac{p_{r^{*}}\pm p_{t}}{p_{t}},

from which we directly get the third inequality since
2p_{v}=p_{t}+p_{r^{*}}
. For the second one, we assume that
r\leq 2.7M
since
p_{N}=p_{t}
otherwise. From the last equality, one gets

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}\Big(\,\frac{2r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}p_{u}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{4r(r-3M)p_{u}}{\Omega^{4}}-\frac{4r(r-3M)p_{u}p_{v}}{\Omega^{4}p_{t}}-\bigg((r-3M)\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}+\frac{27M^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}p_{t}-\Phi r^{3}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\bigg)\frac{2|p_{u}|}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}

(50)

\displaystyle=\frac{2(r-3M)|p_{u}|}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\bigg(\frac{2r|p_{u}|}{\Omega^{2}}-\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\bigg)-\frac{54M^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}|p_{u}|}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}+r^{3}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\frac{2|p_{u}|}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\Phi.

By Remark
3.7
, the second factor of the first term in the RHS is bounded by
|p_{t}|
. According to Proposition
5.20
, the last term is bounded by
|p_{N}|
. We then deduce that

\Big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}\Big(\,\frac{2r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}p_{u}\Big)\Big|\lesssim|p_{N}|\qquad\text{on $\{r\leq 2.7M\}$}.

In view of the definition (
17
) of
p_{N}
and Remark
8.2
, it implies the second inequality. Next, for any
1\leq i\leq 3
, there exists a spherical coordinate system
(\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
in which
\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{i}=\partial_{\phi^{\prime}}
, from which we directly deduce
\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{i}(|\not{p}|)=0
. The identities for the complete lifts of the Killing vector fields follow from direct computations.
∎

We recall a standard result, proved for instance in
[
Big23
, Lemma
5.2
]
.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
h:\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a sufficiently regular function. Then,

\forall\,(t,r^{*})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\qquad\bigg\|\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|h|\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}(t,r^{*})\lesssim\sum_{|I|\leq 2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\big|\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}^{I}h\big|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}.

The next step consists in proving the next result.

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
h:\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a sufficiently regular function and

h^{*}(t^{*},r,\theta,\phi,p)=h(t^{*}-2M\log(r-2M),r^{*}(r),\theta,\phi,p).

Let
x\in\mathcal{R}
and
\tau\geq 0
such that
x\in\Sigma_{\tau}
. We have

r(x)^{2}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|h|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\lesssim\sum_{n+q\leq 1}\sum_{|I|\leq 2}\int_{r=r(x)}^{r(x)+M}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\Big|\Big[\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod},n}\partial_{t}^{q}\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}^{I}h\Big]^{*}\Big|\big(t^{*}(x),r,\omega,p\big){\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}r^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}{\mathrm{d}}r.

Remark 8.5
.

The radial domain of the integral could be chosen to be
[r(x)-M,r(x)]
if
r(x)\geq 3M
.

Proof.

It will be convenient to work with
g\coloneqq h/p_{t}
. We have

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\big|g^{*}\big|(t^{*},r,\theta,\phi,p)=\bigg[\,-\frac{2M}{r\Omega^{2}}\partial_{t}|g|+\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{r^{*}}|g|\bigg]\big(t^{*}-2M\log(r-2M),r^{*}(r),\theta,\phi,p\big)

\displaystyle\quad\;=\frac{1}{r}\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}\big(|g|\big)-\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-3M)+2Mr^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\partial_{t}|g|+\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r^{3}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}+\frac{27M^{2}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{5}{2}}}p_{t}-\Phi\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\bigg)\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}|g|,

where the functions in the RHS are evaluated at
(t^{*}-2M\log(r-2M),r^{*}(r),\theta,\phi,p)
.

As
|p_{t}|r^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}=\sin^{-1}(\theta){\mathrm{d}}p_{r^{*}}{\mathrm{d}}p_{\theta}{\mathrm{d}}p_{\phi}
, we have by a one dimensional local Sobolev inequality,

\displaystyle r(x)^{2}\!\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|p_{t}g|\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{r=r(x)}^{r(x)+M}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\big|p_{t}g^{*}\big|\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}r^{2}{\mathrm{d}}r+\int_{r=r(x)}^{r(x)+M}\bigg|\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|p_{t}|\partial_{r}\big(\big|g^{*}\big|\big){\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\bigg|r^{2}{\mathrm{d}}r.

Performing integration by parts in
p_{r^{*}}
and using that
|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-3M)+2Mr^{\frac{1}{2}}
vanishes for
r=2M
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|p_{t}|\partial_{r}\big(\big|g^{*}\big|\big){\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{r}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|p_{t}|\bigg|\Big[\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}g\Big]^{*}-\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}(\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-})g^{*}\bigg|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}+\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|p_{t}|\Big|\big[\partial_{t}g\big]^{*}\Big|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|p_{t}|\big(1+|\Phi\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|+|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Phi|\big)\big|g^{*}\big|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}.

Recall now from Proposition
5.20
that
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Phi\in L^{\infty}_{x,p}(\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{R}))
. We further have
|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|\lesssim r|p_{N}|
since
p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}
and
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}
are both smooth up to
{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
. This can also be obtained by using Remark
3.7
. Consequently,

\big|\Phi\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{|p_{t}|}|\Phi|\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\big|\lesssim\frac{|p_{N}|}{|p_{t}|}|r\Phi|<+\infty.

Finally, as
h=p_{t}g
, we get from Lemma
8.1

|p_{t}|\bigg|\Big[\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}g\Big]^{*}-\frac{r-3M}{r^{2}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}(\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-})g^{*}\bigg|\lesssim\bigg|\Big[\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}h\Big]^{*}\bigg|+\big|h^{*}\big|

and it remains to apply the Sobolev inequality of Lemma
8.3
.
∎

It allows us to deduce the next result. For convenience, with
n_{\{t^{*}=\textrm{cst}\}}
the normal of a hypersurface of constant
t^{*}
, we define

(51)

p_{t^{*}}\coloneqq p\big(n_{\{t^{*}=\textrm{cst}\}}\big)=\Big(1+\frac{2M}{r}\Big)^{-\frac{1}{2}}p_{v}+\Big(1+\frac{2M}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{p_{u}}{\Omega^{2}}.

Note in particular that
p_{t^{*}}\sim p_{N}
. We will also denote by
{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\{t^{*}=\textrm{cst}\}}
the induced volume form on the hypersurface
\{t^{*}=\textrm{cst}\}
.

Corollary 8.6
.

Let
g:\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a sufficiently regular function,
\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
and
x\in\Sigma_{\tau}
. Then,

•

if
r(x)\leq R_{0}
, we have

r(x)^{2}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}|p_{N}|^{2}|g|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\lesssim\sum_{n+q\leq 1}\sum_{|I|\leq 2}\mathbb{E}\big[p_{N}\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod},n}\partial_{t}^{q}\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}^{I}g\big](\tau).

•

Otherwise
r(x)\geq R_{0}
and we have, for
p\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\square\qquad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle r(x)^{2+p}\!\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\frac{p}{2}}|p_{N}|^{2}|g|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim\sum_{n+q\leq 1}\sum_{|I|\leq 2}\int_{\{t^{*}=t^{*}(x),\,0\leq r(x)-r\leq M\}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}r^{p}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\frac{p}{2}}|p_{t^{*}}|\Big|p_{N}\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod},n}\partial_{t}^{q}\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}^{I}g\Big|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\{t^{*}=t^{*}(x)\}}.

Proof.

We apply the previous Proposition
8.4
to
h=|p_{N}|^{2}g
or
h=r^{p}|p_{v}/p_{t}|^{p/2}|p_{N}|^{2}g
and we use
\partial_{t}(p_{N})=0
,
\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{i}(p_{N})=0
,
\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}(p_{N})\big|\lesssim|p_{N}|
as well as
\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}\big(r^{p}|p_{v}/p_{t}|^{p/2}\big)\big|\lesssim r^{p}|p_{v}/p_{t}|^{p/2}
, which follow from Lemma
8.1
. Note that if
r(x)\geq 3M
, we also use Remark
8.5
. Finally, we exploit
p_{N}\sim p_{t^{*}}
and that
\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R_{0}\}
is equal to
\{t^{*}=\tau,\;r\leq R_{0}\}
.
∎

In order to be able to exploit this Sobolev inequality for
r(x)\geq R_{0}
, we will make use of the next result.

Lemma 8.7
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
and
f
be a solution to
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(f)=0
. For all
\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
and
x\in\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\}
, we have

\sum_{n\leq 1}\int_{\{t^{*}=t^{*}(x),\,0\leq r(x)-r\leq M\}}\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}r^{p}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t}}\Big|^{\frac{p}{2}}|p_{t^{*}}|\Big|p_{N}\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod},n}f\Big|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\{t^{*}=t^{*}(x)\}}\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{p}[f](\tau).

Proof.

Let
y\in\mathcal{R}
such that
(t^{*}(y),\theta(y),\phi(y))=(t^{*}(x),\theta(x),\phi(x))
and
r(y)=r(x)-M
. Then, there exists
\tau^{\prime}\geq\tau
such that
y\in\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}
. We then consider the domain
\mathcal{D}
with boundary

\{t^{*}=t^{*}(x),\,0\leq r(x)-r\leq M\},\qquad\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r\leq r(x)-M\},\qquad\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap\{\tau\leq t^{*}\leq\tau^{\prime}\},\qquad\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r(x)\}.

Recall now the functions
g_{k}
,
1\leq k\leq 7
and Proposition
6.22
. In particular,
g_{7}\sim p_{N}\boldsymbol{V}_{\,+}^{\mathrm{mod}}f
. Let further
g_{0}\coloneqq\xi f
and
c_{0}=1
. Then,

•

if
p=0
, we use Proposition
6.22
and the divergence theorem, applied in
\mathcal{D}
to
\mathrm{N}[c_{k}g_{k}]
.

•

If
p\geq 1
, we apply the divergence theorem to

\mathrm{N}\bigg[c_{k}\overline{\chi}(r)r^{p}\Big|\frac{p_{v}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\Big|^{\frac{p}{2}}g_{k}\bigg]

and we use Corollary
3.15
. The error terms arising from the cutoff function
\overline{\chi}
are handled through the ILED of Proposition
6.2
since
\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{D})\subset\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{P})
.

∎

We are now able to get the main result of this section, from which one can obtain decay estimates for the null components of the energy-momentum tensor
\mathrm{T}[f]
. In particular, by using Proposition
7.3
(respectively Proposition
7.5
), one obtains Corollary
1.1
(respectively Corollary
1.4
).

Corollary 8.8
.

Let
f:\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a solution to the massless Vlasov equation. Then, for all
x\in\mathcal{R}
,

\mathrm{T}_{NN}\big[|f|\big](x)\lesssim\frac{1}{r(x)^{2}}\sum_{q+|I|\leq 3}\mathcal{E}\big[\partial_{t}^{q}\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}^{I}f\big]\big(\tau(x)\big).

Consider futher
p\in\mathbb{N}
. We have

\int_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\frac{|p_{v}|^{p}}{|p_{t}|^{p}}|f||p_{N}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{P}_{x}}\lesssim\frac{1}{r(x)^{2}\langle\tau(x)+r(x)\rangle^{p}}\sum_{q+|I|\leq 3}\mathcal{E}^{p}\big[\partial_{t}^{q}\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}^{I}f\big](0).

Proof.

For the first estimate, we apply the previous Corollary
8.6
and Lemma
8.7
to
\partial_{t}^{q}\widehat{\mathbf{\Omega}}^{I}f
, for any
q+|I|\leq 3
. For the second one we further use Proposition
7.3
providing the decay of the energy flux.
∎

Appendix A
Uniform bounds for the correction term

In this appendix, we prove the next result which implies Proposition
5.20
.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
\Psi\coloneqq(r+6M)\Phi
and
0<\delta\leq 1
. Then, we have

\sup_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}\geq 0\})}\,\frac{|p_{N}|+r^{2}|p_{v}|}{|p_{t}|}|\Psi|+\frac{1}{r}\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|<+\infty.

Remark A.2
.

In view of Remark
3.7
, we have
|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|\lesssim r^{2}|p_{v}|+|p_{t}|
for
r\geq 3M
. As
|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|\lesssim|p_{N}|
for
r\leq 3M
, it implies
|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{\delta}|p_{t}|^{1-\delta}\lesssim|p_{N}|+r^{2}|p_{v}|
for any
0\leq\delta\leq 1
.

A.1.
Preliminaries

The study of
\Psi
and its derivatives will be based on
L^{\infty}_{x,p}
estimates relying on Duhamel formula (see Lemma
A.4
below). For this purpose, we need to introduce the flow map defined by
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}
.

Definition A.3
.

For
\tau^{*}_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
y\in\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=\tau_{0}^{*}\})
, we define

\tau^{*}\mapsto\Phi_{\tau^{*}}(\tau_{0}^{*},y)

to be the flow of
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}
parametrised by
t^{*}
, so that
\Phi_{\tau_{0}^{*}}(\tau_{0}^{*},y)=y
.

We also recall from (
51
) the notation
p_{t^{*}}
for the contraction of
p
with the normal of a hypersurface of constant
t^{*}
. It verifies
p_{t^{*}}\sim p_{N}
.

We now prove a general estimate for a solution to a damped massless Vlasov equation.

Lemma A.4
.

Let
\tau_{1}^{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and let
\Xi\colon\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a function such that

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\Xi)+\boldsymbol{d}(x,p)\Xi=\boldsymbol{s}(x,p),\qquad\qquad\sup_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=\tau_{1}^{*}\})}\big|\Xi\big|<+\infty,

where the damping term
\boldsymbol{d}\colon\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
and the source term
\boldsymbol{s}\colon\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
verify

\forall\,(x,p)\in\mathcal{P},\qquad\big|\boldsymbol{s}(x,p)\big|\lesssim\boldsymbol{d}(x,p).

Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
\boldsymbol{d}
and
\boldsymbol{s}
such that

\forall\,\tau_{2}^{*}\geq\tau_{1}^{*},\qquad\qquad\sup_{\pi^{-1}(\{\tau_{1}^{*}\leq t^{*}\leq\tau_{2}^{*}\})}\big|\Xi\big|\leq\,C\,+\sup_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=\tau_{1}^{*}\})}\big|\Xi\big|.

Proof.

We parametrise the flow of
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}
by the variable
t^{*}
, so we introduce

\underline{\boldsymbol{d}}\coloneqq\frac{\boldsymbol{d}}{|p_{t^{*}}|},\qquad\qquad\underline{\boldsymbol{s}}\coloneqq\frac{\boldsymbol{s}}{|p_{t^{*}}|}.

We further consider
\tau^{*}\geq\tau_{1}^{*}
and
y\in\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=\tau^{*}\})
. According to Duhamel’s formula, we have

\Xi(y)=e^{-\int_{z=\tau_{1}^{*}}^{\tau^{*}}\underline{\boldsymbol{d}}\circ\Phi_{z}(\tau^{*},y){\mathrm{d}}z}\,\Xi\circ\Phi_{\tau_{1}^{*}}(\tau^{*},y)+\int_{q=\tau_{1}^{*}}^{\tau^{*}}e^{-\int_{z=q}^{\tau^{*}}\underline{\boldsymbol{d}}\circ\Phi_{z}(\tau^{*},y){\mathrm{d}}z}\,\underline{\boldsymbol{s}}\circ\Phi_{q}(\tau^{*},y){\mathrm{d}}q.

It remains to remark that, by assumption,

\bigg|\int_{q=\tau_{1}^{*}}^{\tau^{*}}\!e^{-\int_{z=q}^{\tau^{*}}\underline{\boldsymbol{d}}\circ\Phi_{z}(\tau^{*},y){\mathrm{d}}z}\,\underline{\boldsymbol{s}}\circ\Phi_{q}(\tau^{*},y){\mathrm{d}}q\bigg|\lesssim\int_{q=\tau_{1}^{*}}^{\tau^{*}}\!e^{-\int_{z=q}^{\tau^{*}}\underline{\boldsymbol{d}}\circ\Phi_{z}(\tau^{*},y){\mathrm{d}}z}\,\underline{\boldsymbol{d}}\circ\Phi_{q}(\tau^{*},y){\mathrm{d}}q=1-e^{-\int_{z=\tau_{1}^{*}}^{\tau^{*}}\!\underline{\boldsymbol{d}}\circ\Phi_{z}(\tau^{*},y){\mathrm{d}}z}.

∎

A.2.
Pointwise estimate of
\Psi

We compute first the damped Vlasov equation verified by
\Psi=(r+6M)\Phi
.

Lemma A.5
.

The function
\Psi
is uniquely determined as the solution to

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\Psi)+\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\Psi=\boldsymbol{b}(r,p_{t}),\qquad\qquad\Psi|_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=0\})}=0,

where
\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}\colon\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
and
\boldsymbol{b}\colon\mathcal{P}\to{\mathbb{R}}
are given by

\displaystyle\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{r^{2}+2Mr+3M^{2}}{r|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{\frac{3}{2}}+M|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}})}|p_{t}|+\frac{2M}{r^{2}}\frac{|p_{u}|}{\Omega^{2}}-\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{r+6M},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{b}(r,p_{t})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{(r+3M)|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Moreover, they satisfy the bounds

\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\geq\frac{6M|p_{t}|}{r|r+6M|+r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{2M}{r^{2}}\frac{|p_{u}|}{\Omega^{2}},\qquad\quad\big|\boldsymbol{b}(r,p_{t})\big|\leq\frac{2}{r^{2}}|p_{t}|.

Proof.

Recall from Definition
5.18
the equation verified by
\Omega\Phi
. Then, using the computation performed in (
23
), we have
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\Phi)+\boldsymbol{a}\Phi=(r+6M)^{-1}\boldsymbol{b}
. The first part of the statement then follows from
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(r+6M)=p_{r^{*}}
. Next, as
r^{2}+2Mr+3M^{2}\geq r^{2}+Mr^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
|p_{r^{*}}|\leq|p_{t}|
, we have

\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\geq\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{|p_{t}|}{r+6M}+\frac{2M}{r^{2}}\frac{|p_{u}|}{\Omega^{2}},

implying the lower bound for
\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}
. The estimate for
\boldsymbol{b}
follows from
r+3M\leq 2r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}
.
∎

We are now able to control
\Psi
pointwise.

Proposition A.6
.

There holds

\sup_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}\geq 0\})}\,\frac{|p_{N}|}{|p_{t}|}|\Psi|+\frac{r^{2}|p_{v}|}{|p_{t}|}|\Psi|<+\infty.

Proof.

Let us recall from Proposition
3.4
the properties of the weight
\xi
capturing the redshift effect. In particular we will use that
\xi\sim p_{N}
and
-{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(|\xi|)\geq 0
. Thus, we have

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\,\frac{|\xi|}{|p_{t}|}\Psi\Big)+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\frac{|\xi|}{|p_{t}|}\Psi=\frac{|\xi|}{|p_{t}|}\boldsymbol{b}(r,p_{t}),

where, using
|p_{N}|\sim|p_{t}|+\Omega^{-2}|p_{u}|
and Lemma
A.5
,

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\coloneqq\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})-|\xi|^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(|\xi|)\geq\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\gtrsim\frac{|p_{N}|}{r^{2}}.

Consequently, we have

\frac{|\xi|}{|p_{t}|}\big|\boldsymbol{b}(r,p_{t})\big|\lesssim\frac{|p_{N}|}{r^{2}}\lesssim\widetilde{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t}).

The first estimate ensues from Lemma
A.4
, since
\Psi
vanishes initially. Note that we only need to prove the second one for the region
\{r\geq 7M\}
. Recall the cutoff function
\overline{\chi}\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
which satisfies
\overline{\chi}(s)=0
for
s\leq 4M
and
\overline{\chi}(s)=1
for
s\geq 7M
. Then, by Lemma
3.14
, we have

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\overline{\chi}(r)\frac{r^{2}|p_{v}|}{\Omega^{2}|p_{t}|}\Psi\Big)+\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\frac{|\xi|}{|p_{t}|}\Psi=\widehat{\boldsymbol{b}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t}),

where

\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\coloneqq\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})+2\overline{\chi}(r)\frac{r-3M}{\Omega^{4}|p_{t}|}|p_{v}|^{2},\qquad\quad\widehat{\boldsymbol{b}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\coloneqq p_{r^{*}}\overline{\chi}^{\prime}(r)\frac{r^{2}|p_{v}|}{\Omega^{2}|p_{t}|}\Psi+\overline{\chi}(r)\frac{r^{2}|p_{v}|}{\Omega^{2}|p_{t}|}\boldsymbol{b}(r,p_{t}).

In particular, by support considerations and the boundednes of
\Psi
,

\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{b}}\big|(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\lesssim|p_{v}|\mathds{1}_{r\geq 4M}.

As
\widehat{\boldsymbol{a}}\geq 0
, the result would follow from Duhamel formula if we could prove that

\forall\,y\in\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=0\}),\qquad\quad\int_{s=0}^{+\infty}\bigg[\frac{p_{v}}{p_{t^{*}}}\mathds{1}_{r\geq 4M}\bigg]\circ\Phi_{s}(0,y){\mathrm{d}}s\leq C,

for a constant
C>0
independent of
y
. For this, one can simply exploit that, according to Proposition
3.4
and Corollary
3.15
, for
A>0
large enough,

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(A\xi+\overline{\chi}(r)\frac{r^{2}|p_{v}|}{\Omega^{2}|p_{t}|}\Psi\Big)\lesssim-A\frac{|r-3M|}{r^{3}}|p_{t}|+|p_{t}|\mathds{1}_{r\leq 7M}-r|p_{v}|\mathds{1}_{r\geq 7M}\lesssim-|p_{v}|\mathds{1}_{r\geq 4M}.

∎

A.3.
Boundedness for the derivatives of
\Psi

The analysis performed to estimate derivatives of
\Psi
shares some similarities with the one carried out in Section
6
. However, since we will control
L^{\infty}_{x,p}
norms of the derivatives of
\Psi
along null geodesics, we are able to separate the estimates in the different regions of the null-shell in a cleaner way. We recall that the final goal consists in estimating
r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi
.

A.3.1.
Preparatory results

We will have to commute the damped massless Vlasov equation satisfied by
\Psi
, by the vector fields
\partial_{t}
,
{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}
,
\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}
and
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}
. For this, we will use that

(52)

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(Z\Psi\big)+\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})Z\Psi=[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},Z](\Psi)-Z\big[\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\big]\Psi+Z\big[\boldsymbol{b}(r,p_{t})\big],

for any vector field
Z
. Then, we estimate
Z\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}
and
Z\boldsymbol{b}
for all the derivatives that we will consider.

Lemma A.7
.

There holds

\partial_{t}\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}={\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}=\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{b}={\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}\boldsymbol{b}=0.

Moreover, the following estimates hold,

\displaystyle\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\big[\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\big]\Psi\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|p_{t}|}{r},\qquad\qquad\qquad\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\big[\boldsymbol{b}(r,p_{t})\big]\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{2}},

\displaystyle\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{+}\big[\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\big]\Psi\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|p_{t}|}{r},\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{+}\big[\boldsymbol{b}(r,p_{t})\big]\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|p_{N}|}{r^{2}}.

Proof.

The first identities follow from the relations
Z(p_{r^{*}})=Z(p_{t})=Z(r)=0
for
Z\in\{\partial_{t},{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}\}
. Next, we recall from (
49
)–(
50
), where we formally set
\Phi=0
, that

\displaystyle\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}(p_{t})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim|p_{N}|,\qquad\qquad\Big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Big(\,\frac{2r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}p_{u}\Big)\Big|\lesssim r^{2}|p_{N}|,\qquad\qquad\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}(r)=r.

We further note that

p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}p_{t}=p_{r^{*}},\qquad\qquad p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}p_{r^{*}}=p_{t},\qquad\qquad\Big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\frac{p_{u}}{\Omega^{2}}\Big|\leq\frac{|p_{u}|}{\Omega^{2}}\lesssim|p_{N}|.

It allows to obtain the upper bounds for the derivatives of
\boldsymbol{b}
. Using in addition
|p_{N}\Psi|\lesssim|p_{t}|
, we get the estimates related to
\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}
.
∎

For the derivatives
\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi
and
r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi
, the analysis will be divided in three regions,
\{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0,\;r\geq R\}
,
\{r\leq R\}
and
\{p_{r^{*}}\geq 0,r\geq R\}
, where we recall that
R\geq 832M
. This separation is allowed by the properties of the null geodesic flow in Schwarzschild stated in the next lemma. See for instance
[
O’N83
, Chapter 13]
for more information.

Lemma A.8
.

Let
y\in\{t^{*}=0\}
. There exists
0\leq t^{*}_{1}(y)\leq t_{2}^{*}(y)\leq+\infty
such that

(a)

t_{1}^{*}(y)<+\infty
and

•

for all
s^{*}\in(0,t^{*}_{1}(y))
, we have
\Phi_{s^{*}}(0,y)\in\{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0,\;r\geq R\}
,

•

for all
s^{*}>t^{*}_{1}(y)
, there holds
\Phi_{s^{*}}(0,y)\notin\{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0,\;r\geq R\}
.

(b)

t_{2}^{*}(y)
is infinite if and only if the orbit
s^{*}\mapsto\Phi_{s^{*}}(0,y)
is future trapped and

•

for all
s^{*}\in(t^{*}_{1}(y),t_{2}^{*}(y))
, we have
\Phi_{s^{*}}(0,y)\in\{r\leq R\}
.

•

If
t^{*}_{2}(y)<+\infty
, either the orbit
s^{*}\mapsto\Phi_{s^{*}}(0,y)
crosses the future event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
at
t^{*}_{2}(y)
or
\Phi_{s^{*}}(0,y)\in\{p_{r^{*}}\geq 0,\;r\geq R\}
for all
s^{*}\geq t^{*}_{2}(y)
.

For our purposes, we also need to control the derivatives of
\Psi
on the hypersurface
\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=0\})
.

Lemma A.9
.

The following estimates hold,

\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{|p_{t}|}|\partial_{t}\Psi|\Big|_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=0\})}\lesssim\frac{1}{r},\qquad\!\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|\Big|_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=0\})}\lesssim\frac{|p_{t}|}{r|p_{N}|},\qquad\!{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}\Psi\Big|_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=0\})}=p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\Big|_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=0\})}=0.

Proof.

Since the (trivial) data for
\Psi
are prescribed on
\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=0\})
, it is convenient to work in the coordinate system
\mathscr{C}\coloneqq(t^{*},r,\theta,\varphi,p_{r},p_{\theta},p_{\varphi})
of
\mathcal{P}
induced by the coordinate system
(t^{*},r,\theta,\varphi)
on Schwarzschild. We note that

t^{*}=t+2M\log(r-2M),\qquad\qquad p_{r}=\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}}-\frac{2Mp_{t}}{r\Omega^{2}}=\frac{p_{r^{*}}-p_{t}}{\Omega^{2}}+p_{t}.

In order to avoid any confusion, we will denote by
\partial^{*}_{a}
the derivative according to the variable
a
of the coordinate system
\mathscr{C}
. For any
A\in\{\theta,\varphi\}
, we have

\partial^{*}_{t^{*}}=\partial_{t}^{\mathstrut},\qquad\partial^{*}_{p_{r}}=\frac{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}{p_{t}-\frac{2M}{r}p_{r^{*}}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}^{\mathstrut},\qquad\partial^{*}_{A}=\partial_{A}^{\mathstrut},\qquad\partial^{*}_{p_{A}}=\partial_{p_{A}}^{\mathstrut}.

For the radial derivative, the computations are slightly more complicated and we will prove

(53)

\partial^{*}_{r}=-\frac{2M}{r\Omega^{2}}\partial_{t}+\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{r^{*}}-\frac{2Mp_{u}}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}-\frac{6Mp_{v}}{r^{2}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+\frac{2Mp_{r^{*}}(p_{u}+3\Omega^{2}p_{v})}{r^{2}(p_{t}-p_{r^{*}}+\Omega^{2}p_{r^{*}})}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

For this, note first that

\partial_{r}=\frac{2M}{r\Omega^{2}}\partial^{*}_{t^{*}}+\partial^{*}_{r}-\frac{2M(p_{r^{*}}-p_{t})}{r^{2}\Omega^{4}}\partial^{*}_{p_{r}}-\frac{2M}{r\Omega^{2}}\partial_{r}(p_{t})\partial^{*}_{p_{r}}.

Next, we have using the null-shell relation (
16
) that

\partial_{r}(p_{t})=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{2}}\bigg)\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{p_{t}}=-\frac{(r-3M)|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{4}p_{t}}=-\frac{(r-3M)(p_{t}-p_{r^{*}})(p_{t}+p_{r^{*}})}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}p_{t}}.

It implies, as
p_{v}=p_{t}+p_{r^{*}}
and
p_{u}=p_{t}-p_{r^{*}}
,

\partial_{r}=\frac{2M}{r\Omega^{2}}\partial^{*}_{t^{*}}+\partial^{*}_{r}+\frac{4M|p_{u}|^{2}}{r^{2}\Omega^{4}p_{t}}\partial^{*}_{p_{r}}+\frac{12Mp_{u}p_{v}}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\partial^{*}_{p_{r}},

from which we get (
53
).

By definition
\Psi|_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=0\})}=0
, so we have
\partial^{*}_{a}\Psi|_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=0\})}=0
for any variable
a\neq t^{*}
of the coordinate system
\mathscr{C}
. Using the damped massless Vlasov equation verified by
\Psi
, we then have

-\frac{p_{t}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{t}\Psi+\frac{2Mp_{r^{*}}}{r\Omega^{2}}\partial_{t}\Psi=\boldsymbol{b}(r,p_{t})\qquad\text{on $\,\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=0\})$}.

Since

-\frac{p_{t}}{\Omega^{2}}+\frac{2Mp_{r^{*}}}{r\Omega^{2}}=|p_{t}|+\frac{4M|p_{u}|}{r\Omega^{2}}\sim|p_{N}|,

and
|\boldsymbol{b}(r,p_{t})|\lesssim r^{-2}|p_{t}|
, we have

|\partial_{t}\Psi|\Big|_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}\geq 0\})}\lesssim\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{2}|p_{N}|}.

We then get the result by using
|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|\lesssim r|p_{N}|
and that, on
\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=0\})
,

\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi=\frac{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}(r-3M)\partial_{t}\Psi+\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}\cdot\frac{2M}{r}\partial_{t}\Psi=\frac{27M^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}-|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-3M)}\partial_{t}\Psi-r\partial_{t}\Psi.

∎

A.3.2.
The case of the Killing vector fields

As a starting point, we treat the case of the derivatives associated to the symmetries of Schwarzschild.

Proposition A.10
.

We have
{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}(\Psi)=0
and

\sup_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}\geq 0\})}\,\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{|p_{t}|}\big|\partial_{t}\Psi\big|<+\infty.

Proof.

For
Z\in\{\partial_{t},{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}\}
, we have
[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},Z]=0
. Hence, we get from (
52
) and Lemma
A.7
that

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(Z\Psi)+\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})Z\Psi=0.

As
{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}(\Psi)=0
initially according to Lemma
A.9
, we get
{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}(\Psi)=0
. For
\partial_{t}\Psi
, the Lemmata
3.8
and
A.9
provide

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\,\frac{\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}}{p_{t}}\partial_{t}\Psi\,\Big)+\big[\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}+\boldsymbol{a}\big](r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\frac{\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}}{p_{t}}\partial_{t}\Psi=0,\qquad\qquad\sup_{\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}\geq 0\})}\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{|p_{t}|}|\partial_{t}\Psi|<+\infty.

We conclude the proof by using Duhamel’s formula through Lemma
A.4
.
∎

A.3.3.
Radial derivatives along trajectories of incoming far-away particles

We now control the derivatives of
\Psi
along the flow
s^{*}\mapsto\Phi_{s^{*}}(0,y)
until time
t^{*}_{1}(y)
.

Proposition A.11
.

Let
y\in\{t^{*}=0\}
. There exists an absolute constant
B_{1}>0
such that

\sup_{0<t^{*}<t^{*}_{1}(y)}\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|\circ\Phi_{t^{*}}(0,y)+\big|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|\circ\Phi_{t^{*}}(0,y)\leq B_{1}.

Proof.

We first recall that in
\{p_{r^{*}}\leq 0,\;r\geq R\}
, we have
\Omega^{-2}\sim 1
and
|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|\sim r|p_{t}|
. Hence, in this region,

\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|+\big|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|\sim\big|S\Psi\big|+\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|+\big|\partial_{t}\Psi\big|,

so, in view of the previous Proposition
A.10
, it is enough to control
S\Psi
and
p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi
. By following the analysis of Section
6.2
, which allowed us to obtain (
35
), one can prove

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\omega|S\Psi|)+\frac{1}{16}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\omega\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|\big)\leq-\omega\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\Big[\big|S\Psi\big|+\frac{1}{16}\big|p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|\Big]+C\frac{|p_{N}|}{r},

where the weight
\omega
is defined in (
34
). The differences in the analysis are the following:

(a)

According to Proposition
A.10
, we have
{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}(\Psi)=0
and
|p_{v}||\partial_{t}\Psi|\lesssim|p_{t}|r^{-1}
in the region considered. For the second estimate, we also use
|p_{t}|\lesssim r^{-1}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|
, which holds in
\{r\geq 4M,\;p_{r^{*}}\leq 0\}
.

(b)

Here we have
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\Psi)\neq 0
, whereas we had
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(f)=0
in Section
6.2
. The extra (bad) error terms are handled by applying Lemma
A.7
.

(c)

We simply bound above by
0
certain good error terms related to
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\omega)
.

Note now that
\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\!\gtrsim r^{-1}|p_{N}|
since
p_{r^{*}}\!\leq 0
in this region. As
\omega\sim 1
, it remains to use Lemma
A.4
.
∎

A.3.4.
Radial derivatives along trajectories located in the bounded region

We now deal with the bounded region
\{r\leq R\}
, that is, we focus on the time interval
(t_{1}^{*}(y),t_{2}^{*}(y))
. On this domain, there holds
2M\leq r\leq R
, so we can work with multiples of
\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}
and
r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}
. We recall the function
\boldsymbol{a}
introduced in Lemma
3.8
.

Lemma A.12
.

There exists an absolute constant
C_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\big|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|\big)+\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\big|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|+C_{0}r|p_{N}|,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|\big)+\big[\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}+\boldsymbol{a}\big](r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|+C_{0}\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}.

Proof.

From (
52
) applied to
Z=r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}
and Lemma
A.7
, there exists
C_{1}>0
such that

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi|\big)+\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi|\leq\big|[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}](\Psi)\big|+C_{1}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|.

Next, we apply the commutation formula of Proposition
5.15
together with Lemma
A.10
to get

\big|\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\big]\Psi\big|\leq\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|^{2}}{|p_{t}|}\big|\partial_{t}\Psi\big|+\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|\leq C_{1}|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|+\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|.

It remains to use
|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|\lesssim r|p_{N}|
. We now prove the second estimate. Let us recall the commutator
[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}]
from Lemma
5.21
. Applying (
52
) to
Z=\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}
and Lemma
A.7
, we then get

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|\big)+\big[\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}+\boldsymbol{a}\big](r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|\leq\frac{r+3M}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{3}{2}}}|p_{t}|\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|+2\big|{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\Psi)\big|+C_{1}\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}.

Finally, we use
|{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\Psi)|\lesssim r^{-1}|p_{t}|
, which follows from Lemma
A.5
and
|\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}\Psi|\lesssim r^{-1}|p_{N}\Psi|\lesssim r^{-1}|p_{t}|
.
∎

As in Section
6.3
, we use the weight
\zeta^{A}
with
A
large enough, in order to absorb the bad error terms away from the future event horizon
{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
and the photon sphere
\{r=3M\}
.

Corollary A.13
.

There exists an absolute constant
c_{0}>0
such that for all
A\geq 0
, we have on
\{r\leq R\}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\zeta^{A}|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi|\big)+\Big(c_{0}A|p_{r^{*}}|+c_{0}A|r-3M||p_{t}|+\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\Big)\zeta^{A}|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi|\leq\zeta^{A}\big|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|+C_{0}\zeta^{A}r|p_{N}|

and

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\zeta^{A}\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|\big)+\Big(c_{0}A|p_{r^{*}}|+c_{0}A|r-3M||p_{t}|+\big[\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}+\boldsymbol{a}\big](r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\Big)\zeta^{A}\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\zeta^{A}\big|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|+C_{0}\zeta^{A}\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}.

Proof.

Let us recall from Lemma
6.17
that
\zeta\sim 1
and
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(\zeta)\lesssim-|p_{r^{*}}|-|r-3M||p_{t}|
on
\{r\leq R\}
. The result then follows from Lemma
A.12
.
∎

We are now able to conclude the analysis in the bounded region.

Proposition A.14
.

Let
y\in\{t^{*}=0\}
. There exists an absolute constant
B_{2}>0
such that

\sup_{0\leq t^{*}<t^{*}_{2}(y)}\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|\circ\Phi_{t^{*}}(0,y)+\big|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|\circ\Phi_{t^{*}}(0,y)\leq B_{2}.

Proof.

We assume that
t_{2}^{*}(y)>t_{1}^{*}(y)
, otherwise there is nothing to prove. We claim that for
A>0
large enough, there exists
c>0
such that we have on
\{r\leq R\}
,

(54)

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\frac{\zeta^{A}}{8M^{2}}|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi|\bigg)+{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\zeta^{A}\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|\big)+c|p_{N}|\bigg(\frac{\zeta^{A}}{8M^{2}}|r\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi|+\zeta^{A}\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|\bigg)\lesssim|p_{N}|.

Then, the result will be a direct consequence of Proposition
A.11
(providing the estimates up to time
t_{1}^{*}(y)
),
\zeta\sim 1
, and Lemma
A.4
.

We now prove (
54
). For this, we remark that according to Corollary
A.13
and since
\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\gtrsim|p_{N}|r^{-2}
, it suffices to show that if
A>0
is large enough, then

(55)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8M^{2}}\Big(c_{0}A|p_{r^{*}}|+c_{0}A|r-3M||p_{t}|+\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\Big)

\displaystyle>\frac{5}{4}\cdot\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(56)

\displaystyle c_{0}A|p_{r^{*}}|+c_{0}A|r-3M||p_{t}|+\big[\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}+\boldsymbol{a}\big](r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})

\displaystyle>\frac{3}{2}\cdot\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{8M^{2}}.

We introduce
\epsilon_{0}>0
, which will be fixed small enough and we recall

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{(r^{2}+2Mr+3M^{2})|p_{t}|}{r|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r^{\frac{3}{2}}+M|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}})}+\frac{2M|p_{u}|}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}},\qquad\qquad\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})=\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})-\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{r+6M}.

We deal first with (
55
) and we consider two cases:

•

Close to the photon sphere, where
|r-3M|<\epsilon_{0}
. We have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{a}(3M,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{2|p_{t}|}{3(\sqrt{3}+1)M}+\frac{2|p_{u}|}{3M},

\displaystyle\frac{4}{3}\cdot\frac{|p_{t}|}{|3M|^{\frac{5}{2}}|9M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{3}\cdot\frac{|p_{t}|}{27\sqrt{3}M^{3}}\leq\frac{1}{8M^{2}}\cdot\frac{2|p_{t}|}{3(\sqrt{3}+1)M}.

Consequently, if
A
large enough so that
|p_{r^{*}}|(r+6M)^{-1}\leq c_{0}A|p_{r^{*}}|
, we get by continuity that (
55
) holds on
\{|r-3M|<\epsilon_{0}\}
, provided that
\epsilon_{0}
is small enough.

•

The rest of the bounded region,
r\leq R
and
|r-3M|\geq\epsilon_{0}
. In that case, since
|r-3M|
is bounded below, the inequality is verified as soon as
A>0
is large enough.

For the estimate (
56
), we consider again two cases:

•

Close to the future event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
where
2M\leq r<2M+\epsilon_{0}
. By Remark
3.7
, we have

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\square\qquad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{8M^{2}}\leq\frac{2r|p_{u}|}{8M^{2}\Omega^{2}}+\frac{1}{8M^{2}}\cdot\frac{27M^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}-|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-3M)}\leq\Big|1+\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2M}\Big|^{3}\cdot\frac{2M|p_{u}|}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}+\frac{c_{0}}{2}A|r-3M||p_{t}|,

provided that
A>0
is large enough. We get (
56
) if
\epsilon_{0}
is small enough so that
\frac{3}{2}\big|1+\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2M}\big|^{3}\leq 2
.

•

The rest of the bounded region,
2M+\epsilon_{0}\leq r\leq R
. In this domain, we have

\frac{3}{2}\cdot\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{8M^{2}}\lesssim|p_{r^{*}}|+|r-3M||p_{t}|.

We then get (
56
) if
A>0
chosen large enough compared to
\epsilon_{0}^{-1}
.

∎

A.3.5.
Radial derivatives along trajectories of escaping far-away particles

We finally treat the domain
\{p_{r^{*}}\geq 0,\,r\geq R\}
or, equivalently, the time interval
(t_{2}^{*}(y),+\infty)
.

Lemma A.15
.

There exists an absolute constant
C_{0}>0
such that on
\{p_{r^{*}}\geq 0,\,r\geq R\}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\big|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|\big)+\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\big|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{r^{2}}\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|+C_{0}\frac{|p_{N}|}{r},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|\big)+\big[\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}+\boldsymbol{a}\big](r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|+C_{0}\frac{|p_{N}|}{r}.

Proof.

As
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(r^{-2})=-2p_{r^{*}}r^{-3}
, we get the result using Lemma
A.12
and that, for
p_{r^{*}}\geq 0
,

\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})+2p_{r^{*}}r^{-1}=\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})-p_{r^{*}}(r+6M)^{-1}+2p_{r^{*}}r^{-1}\geq\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t}).

∎

This lemma allows us to deduce the next result, which in particular implies Proposition
A.1
.

Proposition A.16
.

There exists a constant
B_{3}>0
such that for all
y\in\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=0\})
,

\sup_{t^{*}\geq 0}\,\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|\circ\Phi_{t^{*}}(0,y)+\big|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|\circ\Phi_{t^{*}}(0,y)\leq B_{3}.

Proof.

Let
y\in\pi^{-1}(\{t^{*}=0\})
. We remark that in view of Proposition
A.14
, we only need to treat the time interval
t^{*}\geq t_{2}^{*}(y)
. We then assume that the orbit
\tau\mapsto\Phi_{\tau}(0,y)
escapes to future null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{+}
, since there is nothing to prove otherwise. The key step consists in proving that

(57)

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\frac{3}{2}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi|+|\boldsymbol{V}_{+}\Psi|\bigg)+\frac{|p_{t}|}{4(r+6M)}\bigg(\frac{3}{2}|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi|+|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi|\bigg)\lesssim C_{0}\frac{|p_{t}|}{r}

on
\{p_{r^{*}}\geq 0,\,r\geq R\}
. Indeed, since

\big|\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\Psi\big|\circ\Phi_{t^{*}_{2}(y)}(0,y)+\big|r^{-1}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\Psi\big|\circ\Phi_{t^{*}_{2}(y)}(0,y)\leq B_{2}

by Proposition
A.14
, the result will follow by Lemma
A.4
.

We note now that (
57
) is implied by Lemma
A.15
,
p_{N}=p_{t}
for
r\geq R
and

\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\geq\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}},\qquad\qquad\big[\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}+\boldsymbol{a}\big](r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})\geq\frac{3}{2}\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{r^{2}}+\frac{|p_{t}|}{4(r+6M)}.

The first inequality follows from
r^{2}+2Mr+3M^{2}\geq r^{2}+Mr^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}
. By Remark
3.7
, we have for
r\geq 3M
,

\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{r^{2}}\leq\frac{|p_{t}|-p_{r^{*}}}{r\Omega^{2}}+\frac{27M^{2}|p_{t}|}{r^{3}+r^{\frac{3}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-3M)}\leq\frac{|p_{t}|-p_{r^{*}}}{r\Omega^{2}}+\frac{27M^{2}|p_{t}|}{r^{3}}.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})-\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{r+6M}\geq\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{r+6M}\geq\frac{|p_{t}|-p_{r^{*}}}{r+6M},

so that, for
p_{r^{*}}\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\overline{\boldsymbol{a}}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{r^{2}}-\frac{8M(|p_{t}|-p_{r^{*}})}{(r-2M)(r+6M)}-\frac{27M^{2}|p_{t}|}{r^{3}}\geq\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{r^{2}}-\frac{8M|p_{t}|}{(r-2M)(r+6M)}-\frac{27M^{2}|p_{t}|}{r^{3}}.

Now, still for
p_{r^{*}}\geq 0
and
r\geq 3M
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{|p_{t}|}{2(r+6M)}\geq\frac{|\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}|}{2r^{2}}-\frac{8M|p_{t}|}{2(r-2M)(r+6M)}-\frac{27M^{2}|p_{t}|}{2r^{3}}.

We conclude the proof by noting that for
r\geq R\geq 832M
,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{a}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})-\frac{24M|p_{t}|}{(r-2M)(r+6M)}-\frac{81M^{2}|p_{t}|}{r^{3}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{|p_{t}|}{r+6M}-\frac{24M|p_{t}|}{(r-2M)(r+6M)}-\frac{81M^{2}|p_{t}|}{r^{3}}\geq\frac{|p_{t}|}{2(r+6M)}.

∎

Appendix B
Commutator associated to a conserved quantity arising from trapping

In order to consider symplectic gradients, we cannot merely work on the null-shell
\mathcal{P}
and we need to consider a larger subset of the cotangent bundle, that is the one of the causal geodesics. The goal of this section consists in justifying our choice to work
V_{+}
, which is a particular projection of
{\mathbb{X}}_{\varphi_{-}}
on
T\mathcal{P}
. For this purpose, we recall the notations introduced in Section
2.2
.

We start by defining, for all
m\geq 0
, the mass-shell

\mathcal{P}_{m}=\Big\{(x,p)\in T^{*}\mathcal{S}\;\big|\;g_{x}^{-1}(p,p)=-m^{2},\;\text{ $p$ is future-directed}\Big\},

so that
\mathcal{P}_{0}=\mathcal{P}
. In particular, if
s\mapsto\gamma(s)
is a future-directed geodesic verifying
g(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma})=-m^{2}
, we have for all
s
that

\big(\gamma(s),g_{\gamma(s)}(\dot{\gamma}(s),\cdot)\big)\in\mathcal{P}_{m}.

The relation
g_{x}^{-1}(p,p)=-m^{2}
, often called the mass-shell relation, is equivalent to

(58)

|p_{t}|^{2}=|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}+\Omega^{2}\Big(m^{2}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big).

The mass-shell
\mathcal{P}_{m}
can be parametrised by the coordinates
(t,r^{*},\theta,\phi,p_{r^{*}},p_{\theta},p_{\phi})
using (
58
). The geodesic spray, which is tangent to the mass-shell, then reads on
\mathcal{P}_{m}
as

(59)

\mathbb{X}_{g}=-\frac{p_{t}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{t}+\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{r^{*}}+\frac{p_{\theta}}{r^{2}}\partial_{\theta}+\frac{p_{\phi}}{r^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}\partial_{\phi}-\frac{m^{2}Mr^{2}-(r-3M)|\not{p}|^{2}}{r^{4}}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+\frac{\cot\theta}{r^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}p_{\phi}^{2}\partial_{p_{\theta}}.

B.1.
Circular orbits for causal geodesics

Let
m\geq 0
. Since the circular orbits for causal geodesics lie in
\{p_{r^{*}}=0\}
, the possible orbits associated to a given angular momentum
|\not{p}|
are the roots of

m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{r-3M}{M}|\not{p}|^{2}=m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{r}{M}|\not{p}|^{2}+3|\not{p}|^{2}.

As a result, we find circular orbits if and only if
|\not{p}|\geq 2\sqrt{3}mM
. They are then located on the spheres
\{r=r_{\pm}^{m}(|\not{p}|)\}
, where

(60)

r_{\pm}^{m}(|\not{p}|)=\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2Mm^{2}}\bigg(1\pm\sqrt{1-\frac{12M^{2}m^{2}}{|\not{p}|^{2}}}\,\bigg).

We remark in particular that

(61)

\hskip-14.22636ptr_{-}^{m}(|\not{p}|)=3M+\frac{9M^{3}m^{2}}{|\not{p}|^{2}}+O_{m\to 0}\big(m^{4}\big),\qquad\qquad\qquad r_{+}^{m}(|\not{p}|)\underset{m\to 0}{\sim}\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{Mm^{2}}.

B.2.
The weight functions
\varphi_{\pm}^{m}

We start by extending the functions
\varphi_{\pm}
, which are defined on the null-shell (see Definition
3.6
and (
24
)). The choice of the extensions of
\varphi_{\pm}
is justified in
[
VR24
, Subsection 5.1.4]
.

Definition B.1
.

Let
m>0
. We define the following quantities on
\mathcal{P}_{m}
:

•

If
|\not{p}|\geq 2\sqrt{3}mM
, we set

a^{m}(|\not{p}|)\coloneqq\frac{2m^{2}M^{2}|r^{m}_{-}(|\not{p}|)|^{3}}{|\not{p}|^{2}(4M-r^{m}_{-}(|\not{p}|))(r^{m}_{-}(|\not{p}|)-3M)}.

•

For
|\not{p}|>4mM
, let
\rho^{m}_{\pm}(|\not{p}|)
be the roots of
m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r+|\not{p}|^{2}
. They are given by

\rho_{\pm}^{m}(|\not{p}|)=\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{4Mm^{2}}\bigg(1\pm\sqrt{1-\frac{16M^{2}m^{2}}{|\not{p}|^{2}}}\,\bigg).

•

We consider, for
|\not{p}|>4mM
and
\rho_{-}^{m}(|\not{p}|)<r<\rho_{+}^{m}(|\not{p}|)
, the functions

\varphi_{\pm}^{m}(x,p)\coloneqq\frac{\sqrt{2M}|\not{p}|r^{\frac{3}{2}}}{(-m^{2}r^{2}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r-|\not{p}|^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg(p_{r^{*}}\pm\Big(1+\frac{a^{m}(|\not{p}|)}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1-\frac{r^{m}_{-}(|\not{p}|)}{r}\Big)\big||p_{t}|^{2}-m^{2}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg).

One can check, using (
61
), that

(62)

a^{m}(|\not{p}|)\underset{m\to 0}{\sim}6M,\qquad\quad\rho^{m}_{-}(|\not{p}|)\underset{m\to 0}{\sim}2M,\qquad\quad\rho^{m}_{+}(|\not{p}|)\underset{m\to 0}{\sim}\frac{r_{+}^{m}}{2},\qquad\quad\varphi_{\pm}^{m}(x,p)\underset{m\to 0}{\sim}\varphi_{\pm}.

We can then set
\rho^{0}_{+}(|\not{p}|)=+\infty
and extend by continuity
a^{m}(|\not{p}|)
,
\rho_{-}^{m}(|\not{p}|)
and
\varphi_{\pm}^{m}
at
m=0
.

Proposition B.2
.

Let
m>0
and
|\not{p}|>4Mm
. We have on
\mathcal{P}_{m}
and for
\rho_{-}^{m}(|\not{p}|)<r<\rho_{+}^{m}(|\not{p}|)
,

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\varphi_{\pm}^{m}\big)=\pm\frac{m^{2}(|p_{t}|^{2}-m^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r^{m}_{+}(|\not{p}|))}{2(r+a^{m}(|\not{p}|))^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r+|\not{p}|^{2}\big)}\varphi_{\pm}^{m}.

Remark B.3
.

In the limit
m\to 0
, we recover the relation satisfied by
\varphi_{\pm}=\varphi^{0}_{\pm}
on the null-shell (see Lemmata
3.8
and
3.10
). Indeed, by (
62
), we have

\frac{m^{2}(|p_{t}|^{2}-m^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r^{m}_{+}(|\not{p}|))}{2(r+a^{m}(|\not{p}|))^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r+|\not{p}|^{2}\big)}\underset{m\to 0}{\sim}\frac{|p_{t}|r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-2M)}=\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M)|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}.

Proof.

In order to lighten the notations, we drop the dependence in
|\not{p}|
of
r_{\pm}^{m}
and
a^{m}
. We first compute, using
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(|\not{p}|)=0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\Big(\Big|-m^{2}r^{2}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r-|\not{p}|^{2}\Big|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{r^{*}}\big(2m^{2}r-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}\big)}{2\big|-m^{2}r^{2}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r-|\not{p}|^{2}\big|^{\frac{3}{2}}},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(r^{\frac{3}{2}}p_{r^{*}}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}-\frac{M}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}}\Big(m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{M}r+3|\not{p}|^{2}\Big),

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big((r+a^{m})^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r_{-}^{m})\big)

\displaystyle=p_{r^{*}}\frac{3r-r_{-}^{m}+2a^{m}}{2(r+a^{m})^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\frac{r^{\frac{3}{2}}p_{r^{*}}}{\big|-m^{2}r^{2}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r-|\not{p}|^{2}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\,=\frac{-|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2\big|-m^{2}r^{2}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r-|\not{p}|^{2}\big|^{\frac{3}{2}}}\Big(m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{M}r+3|\not{p}|^{2}\Big)-\frac{1}{\big|-m^{2}r^{2}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r-|\not{p}|^{2}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{M}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}}\Big(m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{M}r+3|\not{p}|^{2}\Big).

We then deduce, using the mass-shell relation (
58
), that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\frac{r^{\frac{3}{2}}p_{r^{*}}}{\big|m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r+|\not{p}|^{2}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=-\frac{m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{M}r+3|\not{p}|^{2}}{\big|-m^{2}r^{2}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r-|\not{p}|^{2}\big|^{\frac{3}{2}}}\cdot\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}\big(|p_{t}|^{2}-m^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{m^{2}\big||p_{t}|^{2}-m^{2}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r^{m}_{+})}{2(r+a^{m})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r+|\not{p}|^{2}\big)}\cdot\frac{(r+a^{m})^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r_{-}^{m})}{\big|m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r+|\not{p}|^{2}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big||p_{t}|^{2}-m^{2}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We also obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\frac{(r+a^{m})^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r_{-}^{m})}{\big|-m^{2}r^{2}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r-|\not{p}|^{2}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg)=\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{2(a^{m}+r)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|-m^{2}r^{2}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r-|\not{p}|^{2}\big|^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\cdot\bigg(\Big(2m^{2}r-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}\Big)(r+a^{m})(r-r^{m}_{-})+(3r+2a^{m}-r^{m}_{-})\Big(-m^{2}r^{2}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r-|\not{p}|^{2}\Big)\bigg).

Now, we claim that

\Big(2m^{2}r-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}\Big)(r+a^{m})(r-r^{m}_{-})-(3r+2a^{m}-r^{m}_{-})\Big(m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r+|\not{p}|^{2}\Big)=-m^{2}r^{3}+m^{2}r^{m}_{+}r^{2}.

For this, note first that the polynomial on the LHS is equal to
-m^{2}r^{3}+b_{2}r^{2}+b_{1}r+b_{0}
, where

\displaystyle b_{0}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}a^{m}r_{-}^{m}-2|\not{p}|^{2}a^{m}+r_{-}^{m}|\not{p}|^{2},\qquad b_{1}\coloneqq-2m^{2}a^{m}r_{-}^{m}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}a^{m}-3|\not{p}|^{2},\qquad b_{2}\coloneqq-m^{2}r_{-}^{m}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{M}.

By (
60
), we have
b_{2}=m^{2}r_{+}^{m}
. Then, recalling first the definition of
a^{m}(|\not{p}|)
and using then the relation
m^{2}|r_{-}^{m}|^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{M}r_{-}^{m}+3|\not{p}|^{2}=0
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{2m^{2}M^{2}|r_{-}^{m}|^{2}}{|\not{p}|^{2}a^{m}}b_{0}

\displaystyle=Mm^{2}|r_{-}^{m}|^{3}-4M^{2}m^{2}|r_{-}^{m}|^{2}+|\not{p}|^{2}(4M-r_{-}^{m})(r_{-}^{m}-3M)=0.

Similarly, one can check that

\displaystyle\frac{2m^{2}M^{2}|r_{-}^{m}|^{3}}{a^{m}}b_{1}

\displaystyle=-4M^{2}m^{4}|r_{-}^{m}|^{4}+|\not{p}|^{2}Mm^{2}|r_{-}^{m}|^{3}+3|\not{p}|^{4}|r_{-}^{m}|^{2}-21|\not{p}|^{4}Mr_{-}^{m}+36|\not{p}|^{4}M^{2}

\displaystyle=-4M^{2}\big(|\not{p}|^{2}M^{-1}r_{-}^{m}-3|\not{p}|^{2}\big)^{2}+|\not{p}|^{2}Mm^{2}|r_{-}^{m}|^{3}+3|\not{p}|^{4}|r_{-}^{m}|^{2}-21|\not{p}|^{4}Mr_{-}^{m}+36|\not{p}|^{4}M^{2}

\displaystyle=|\not{p}|^{2}Mm^{2}|r_{-}^{m}|^{3}-|\not{p}|^{4}|r_{-}^{m}|^{2}+3|\not{p}|^{4}Mr_{-}^{m}=0.

We then deduce that

{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\bigg(\frac{(r+a^{m})^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r_{-}^{m})}{\big|-m^{2}r^{2}+\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r-|\not{p}|^{2}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg)=\frac{m^{2}r^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r^{m}_{+})}{2(a^{m}+r)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r+|\not{p}|^{2}\big)}\cdot\frac{r^{\frac{3}{2}}p_{r^{*}}}{\big|m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r+|\not{p}|^{2}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}},

which allows us to conclude the proof since
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}(|p_{t}|^{2}-m^{2})={\mathbb{X}}_{g}(|\not{p}|)=0
.
∎

In what follows, we denote by
m
the function
m(x,p)\coloneqq|-g_{x}^{-1}(p,p)|^{\frac{1}{2}}
, which is well-defined on the subset
\{g_{x}^{-1}(p,p)\leq 0\}
of the cotangent bundle. Recall that, for
(x,p)\in\mathcal{P}
