, we denote by
\tau\mapsto\Phi_{\tau}(x,p)
the flow map of
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}
parametrised by
t^{*}
with data
\Phi_{t^{*}(x)}(x,p)=(x,p)
. Then, according to the previous Proposition
B.2
and Remark
B.3
, the following statement holds.

Corollary B.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{s}
be the function defined as

\mathfrak{s}(x,p)\coloneqq\varphi_{-}^{m}(x,p)e^{\alpha(x,p)},\qquad\alpha(x,p)\coloneqq\int_{\tau=0}^{t^{*}(x)}\frac{m^{2}(|p_{t}|^{2}-m^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r^{m}_{+}(|\not{p}|))}{2(r+a^{m}(|\not{p}|))^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r+|\not{p}|^{2}\big)}\circ\Phi_{\tau}(x,p){\mathrm{d}}\tau.

Then,
\mathfrak{s}
can be extended on
\mathcal{P}
, that is at
m=0
. Moreover, it is conserved along the causal geodesic flow.

B.3.
Symplectic gradients

We now compute the symplectic gradient of
\varphi_{-}^{m}
. For this, remark that we can write

\varphi_{-}^{m}=\phi_{-}\bigg(m^{2},\frac{m^{2}}{|\not{p}|^{2}},r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t}\bigg),\qquad\qquad\phi_{-}\in C^{1}\big(\mathring{O},{\mathbb{R}}\big),

where the open set
\mathring{O}
is the interior of
O
, given by

O\coloneqq\Big\{(x_{1},\dots,x_{5})\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times\Big[0,\frac{16}{M^{2}}\Big)\times(2M,+\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{-}^{*}\,\big|\,\rho_{-}\big(\sqrt{x_{1}/x_{2}}\,\big)<x_{3}<\rho_{+}\big(\sqrt{x_{1}/x_{2}}\,\big)\Big\}.

Moreover, in view of (
61
),
\phi_{-}
and
\nabla\phi_{-}
can be extended continuously on
O
, so that

\varphi_{-}=\varphi_{-}^{0}=\phi_{-}(0,0,\cdot),\qquad\!\partial_{r^{*}}\varphi_{-}=\Omega^{2}\partial_{x^{3}}\phi_{-}(0,0,\cdot),\qquad\!\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\varphi_{-}=\partial_{x^{4}}\phi_{-}(0,0,\cdot),\qquad\!\partial_{p_{t}}\varphi_{-}=\partial_{x^{5}}\phi_{-}(0,0,\cdot).

Since
m^{2}=2H
, we have

2{\mathbb{X}}_{m^{2}}={\mathbb{X}}_{g}
. By the chain rule, we then obtain

{\mathbb{X}}_{\varphi_{-}^{m}}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x^{1}}\phi_{-}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}+\frac{1}{2|\not{p}|^{2}}\partial_{x^{2}}\phi_{-}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}-2\frac{m^{2}}{|\not{p}|^{3}}\partial_{x^{2}}\phi_{-}{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}+\mathbb{V}_{+},\qquad\mathbb{V}_{+}\coloneqq\partial_{p_{t}}\big(\varphi_{-}^{m}\big)\partial_{t}+\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\big(\varphi_{-}^{m}\big)\partial_{r^{*}}-\partial_{r^{*}}\big(\varphi_{-}^{m}\big)\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}.

Remark B.5
.

We abusively wrote
\partial_{r^{*}}\big(\varphi_{-}^{m}\big)
,
\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\big(\varphi_{-}^{m}\big)
and
\partial_{p_{t}}\big(\varphi_{-}^{m}\big)
instead of
\Omega^{2}\partial_{x^{3}}\phi_{-}
,
\partial_{x^{4}}\phi_{-}
and
\partial_{x^{5}}\phi_{-}
.

For convenience, we introduce

\boldsymbol{x}_{g}(r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t}):=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x^{1}}\phi_{-}(0,0,r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t})+\frac{1}{2|\not{p}|^{2}}\partial_{x^{2}}\phi_{-}(0,0,r,p_{r^{*}},p_{t}).

Then, we have
\mathbb{V}_{+}={\mathbb{X}}_{\varphi_{-}^{m}}-\boldsymbol{x}_{g}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}
on the null-shell
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathbb{V}_{+}
is indeed given by (
10
). Moreover, as
\mathfrak{s}
is a conserved quantity, we have

\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},{\mathbb{X}}_{\mathfrak{s}}\big]=0,\qquad\qquad{\mathbb{X}}_{\mathfrak{s}}=e^{\alpha(x,p)}{\mathbb{X}}_{\varphi_{-}^{m}}+\varphi_{-}^{m}e^{\alpha(x,p)}{\mathbb{X}}_{\alpha}.

We summarise these properties in the next statement, which also uses Remark
B.3
.

Proposition B.6
.

The vector fields
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}
,
{\mathbb{X}}_{|\not{p}|}
, and
{\mathbb{X}}_{\mathfrak{s}}
commute with the geodesic spray
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}
. We have

\forall\,(x,p)\in\mathcal{P},\qquad\alpha|_{\mathcal{P}}(x,p)=\int_{s=0}^{t^{*}(x)}\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\circ\Phi_{s}(x,p){\mathrm{d}}s.

On the null-shell
\mathcal{P}
, we have

\mathbb{V}_{+}=e^{-\alpha|_{\mathcal{P}}(x,p)}{\mathbb{X}}_{\mathfrak{s}}-\boldsymbol{x}_{g}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}-\varphi_{-}{\mathbb{X}}_{\alpha},

where

\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},e^{-\alpha|_{\mathcal{P}}(x,p)}{\mathbb{X}}_{\mathfrak{s}}\big]=-\frac{|p_{t}|}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}e^{-\alpha|_{\mathcal{P}}(x,p)}{\mathbb{X}}_{\mathfrak{s}},\qquad\qquad\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},\boldsymbol{x}_{g}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big]={\mathbb{X}}_{g}\big(\boldsymbol{x}_{g}\big){\mathbb{X}}_{g}.

We then observe that the vector field
\mathbb{V}_{+}
is the sum of

•

e^{-\alpha|_{\mathcal{P}}(x,p)}{\mathbb{X}}_{\mathfrak{s}}
, which enjoys a good commutation relation with
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}
,

•

a term collinear to
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}
, which is then irrelevant once applied to a solution to the Vlasov equation,

•

a term proportional to
\varphi_{-}
.

The issue is that
\mathbb{V}_{+}
is not tangent to
\mathcal{P}
so we need to project it on
T\mathcal{P}
to use it for the study of massless Vlasov fields. However, since the normal to
\mathcal{P}
is also tangent to it, there is no canonical choice of projection. The next result justifies that any choice should allow to close the estimates.

Proposition B.7
.

Let
W
be a vector field transverse to
T\mathcal{P}
and

\mathrm{Proj}_{\parallel W}:\bigcup_{(x,p)\in\mathcal{P}}T_{(x,p)}T^{*}\mathcal{S}\to T\mathcal{P}

be the projection parallel to
W
. Then, for any vector fields
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
transverse to
T\mathcal{P}
, we have

\mathrm{Proj}_{\parallel W_{1}}(\mathbb{V}_{+})-\mathrm{Proj}_{\parallel W_{2}}(\mathbb{V}_{+})=\varphi_{-}\big(\,\mathrm{Proj}_{\parallel W_{2}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\alpha})-\mathrm{Proj}_{\parallel W_{1}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\alpha})\,\big).

Proof.

Since
\mathfrak{s}
is conserved along causal geodesics, the vector field
{\mathbb{X}}_{\mathfrak{s}}
is tangent to
\mathcal{P}
. As
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}|_{\mathcal{P}}\in T\mathcal{P}
, we get that
\mathbb{V}_{+}+\varphi_{-}{\mathbb{X}}_{\alpha}
is tangent to the null-shell as well. Thus,

\mathrm{Proj}_{\parallel W}(\mathbb{V}_{+})+\varphi_{-}\mathrm{Proj}_{\parallel W}({\mathbb{X}}_{\alpha})=\mathbb{V}_{+}+\varphi_{-}{\mathbb{X}}_{\alpha}

for any vector field
W
transverse to
T\mathcal{P}
.
∎

In this article, we choose to work with
V_{+}\!\coloneqq\mathrm{Proj}_{\parallel\partial_{p_{t}}}(\mathbb{V}_{+})
. As suggested by Proposition
B.9
, for certain well-chosen
W_{2}
, the difference
V_{+}-\mathrm{Proj}_{\parallel W_{2}}(\mathbb{V}_{+})
is collinear to
\varphi_{-}p_{t}\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}
. Let us now briefly justify that working with
\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}\!=\Omega^{-1}V_{+}
is a relevant choice. The next result can be obtained similarly as Proposition
B.6
.

Proposition B.8
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}^{m}\coloneqq\Omega^{-1}\varphi_{-}^{m}
. Then, the following properties hold.

•

The quantity
\overline{\mathfrak{s}}(x,p)\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}^{m}(x,p)e^{\overline{\alpha}(x,p)}
, where

\overline{\alpha}(x,p)\coloneqq\int_{\tau=0}^{t^{*}(x)}\bigg(\frac{m^{2}(|p_{t}|^{2}-m^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}}(r-r^{m}_{+}(|\not{p}|))}{2(r+a^{m}(|\not{p}|))^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(m^{2}r^{2}-\frac{|\not{p}|^{2}}{2M}r+|\not{p}|^{2}\big)\Omega}+\frac{M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}p_{r^{*}}\bigg)\circ\Phi_{\tau}(x,p){\mathrm{d}}\tau,

can be extended on
\mathcal{P}
, that is at
m=0
. Furthermore, it is conserved along the geodesic flow.

•

The function
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}
defined on the null-shell in (
22
) and
\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}^{0}
corresponds. Moreover,

\forall\,(x,p)\in\mathcal{P},\qquad\overline{\alpha}(x,p)=\int_{s=0}^{t^{*}(x)}\boldsymbol{a}\circ\Phi_{s}(x,p){\mathrm{d}}s,

where
\boldsymbol{a}
is the function introduced in Lemma
3.8
.

•

Since
\overline{\mathfrak{s}}
is a conserved quantity, its symplectic gradient
{\mathbb{X}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{s}}}=e^{\overline{\alpha}(x,p)}{\mathbb{X}}_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}^{m}}+e^{\overline{\alpha}(x,p)}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}^{m}{\mathbb{X}}_{\overline{\alpha}}
verifies
[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},{\mathbb{X}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{s}}}]=0
. Furthermore, we have on
\mathcal{P}
, in the coordinate system
(t,r^{*},\theta,\phi,p_{t},p_{r^{*}},p_{\theta},p_{\phi})
,

\big[{\mathbb{X}}_{g},e^{-\overline{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{P}}(x,p)}{\mathbb{X}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{s}}}\big]=-\boldsymbol{a}(x,p)e^{-\overline{\alpha}|_{\mathcal{P}}(x,p)}{\mathbb{X}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{s}}}

and, using similar abuse of notations than in Remark
B.5
,

{\mathbb{X}}_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}^{m}}=\partial_{p_{t}}\big(\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\big)\partial_{t}+\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}\big(\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\big)\partial_{r^{*}}-\partial_{r^{*}}\big(\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\big)\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}+\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{g}}{\Omega}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}.

In order to compare
\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}=\Omega^{-1}V_{+}
with a projection of
{\mathbb{X}}_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}^{m}}-\boldsymbol{x}_{g}\Omega^{-1}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}
on
T\mathcal{P}
, we will work in the coordinate system
(\bar{t},r,\theta,\phi,\overline{p}_{t},\overline{p}_{r},p_{\theta},p_{\phi})
of
T^{*}\mathcal{S}
induced by the hyperboloidal coordinates
(\bar{t},r,\theta,\varphi)
, which were introduced in Definition
6.23
. In particular, we have

\bar{t}=t-\int_{3M}^{r}\frac{\xi(s)}{\Omega^{2}(s)}{\mathrm{d}}s,\qquad\quad\xi(r)=\Big(1-\frac{3M}{r}\,\Big)\Big(1+\frac{6M}{r}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},\qquad\quad\overline{p}_{t}=p_{t},\qquad\quad\overline{p}_{r}=\frac{p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}}+\frac{\xi(r)}{\Omega^{2}}p_{t}.

In order to avoid any confusion, we denote by
\overline{\partial}_{a}
the derivative with respect to
a
in this coordinate system. Then, as
\partial_{|\not{p}|}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}^{m}|_{m=0}=0
and since
\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{t}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}^{m}
,
\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}^{m}
and
\overline{\partial}_{r}\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}^{m}
are continous at
m=0
, we have on the null-shell
\mathcal{P}
, with similar abuse of notations than in Remark
B.5
,

\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}=r\overline{p}_{r},\qquad\qquad{\mathbb{X}}_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}^{m}}-\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{g}}{\Omega}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}=\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{t}}\big(\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\big)\overline{\partial}_{\overline{t}}+\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}\big(\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\big)\overline{\partial}_{r}-\overline{\partial}_{r}\big(\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}\big)\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}=r\overline{\partial}_{r}-\overline{p}_{r}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}.

We finally prove the next result.

Proposition B.9
.

We have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Proj}_{\parallel\partial_{\overline{p}_{t}}}\big({\mathbb{X}}_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}^{m}}\big)-\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{g}}{\Omega}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}-\frac{M}{r\Omega^{2}}\overline{p}_{r}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}-\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi(r)}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\overline{p}_{r}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}.

The sum of the last two terms is a vector field regular up to
{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
since
\xi(2M)=-1
.

Proof.

We parametrise the null-shell
\mathcal{P}
by the coordinate system
(\overline{t},r,\theta,\phi,\overline{p}_{r},p_{\theta},p_{\phi})
and we abusively denote by
\overline{\partial}_{a}
the derivative with respect to the variable
a
. Then, as
{\mathbb{X}}_{g}
is tangent to
\mathcal{P}
,

\mathrm{Proj}_{\parallel\partial_{\overline{p}_{t}}}\big({\mathbb{X}}_{\boldsymbol{\varphi}_{-}^{m}}\big)=r\overline{\partial}_{r}-\overline{p}_{r}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}+\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{g}}{\Omega}{\mathbb{X}}_{g}.

We now relate
\overline{\partial}_{a}
with the derivatives of the coordinate system
(t,r^{*},\theta,\phi,p_{r^{*}},p_{\theta},p_{\phi})
. For any
A\in\{\theta,\varphi\}
, we have

\partial_{t}=\overline{\partial}_{\bar{t}},\qquad\quad\partial_{p_{r^{*}}}=\frac{p_{t}+\xi(r)p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}},\qquad\quad\partial_{A}=\overline{\partial}_{A},\qquad\quad\partial_{p_{A}}=\overline{\partial}_{p_{A}}.

For the radial derivative, note first that

\partial_{r}=-\frac{\xi(r)}{\Omega^{2}}\overline{\partial}_{\bar{t}}+\overline{\partial}_{r}-\frac{2M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\cdot\frac{p_{r^{*}}+\xi(r)p_{t}}{\Omega^{2}}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}+\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{r}(\xi(r)p_{t})\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}.

Then, using (
31
) and the null-shell relation (
16
), we have

\partial_{r}(p_{t})=-\frac{(r-3M)(|p_{t}|^{2}-|p_{r^{*}}|^{2})}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}p_{t}},\qquad\qquad\qquad\partial_{r}(\xi(r))=\frac{27M^{2}}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

This implies,

\partial_{r}=-\frac{\xi(r)}{\Omega^{2}}\overline{\partial}_{\bar{t}}+\overline{\partial}_{r}-\frac{2M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}\overline{p}_{r}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}-\frac{|\xi(r)|^{2}(|p_{t}|^{2}-|p_{r^{*}}|^{2})}{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{4}p_{t}}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}+\frac{27M^{2}}{r^{\frac{5}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}.

Recall now from (
29
) the expression of
\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}=\Omega^{-1}V_{+}
. We have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{V}_{\!+}

\displaystyle=r\frac{\xi(r)}{\Omega^{2}}\overline{\partial}_{\bar{t}}+r\partial_{r}-\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r}\overline{p}_{r}+\frac{27M^{2}p_{t}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg)\frac{p_{t}+\xi(r)p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}

\displaystyle=\big(r\overline{\partial}_{r}-\overline{p}_{r}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}\big)+\overline{p}_{r}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}-\frac{2M}{r\Omega^{2}}\cdot\overline{p}_{r}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}-\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|\xi(r)|^{2}(|p_{t}|^{2}-|p_{r^{*}}|^{2})}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{4}p_{t}}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}+\frac{27M^{2}p_{t}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}

\displaystyle\quad-\bigg(\frac{r-3M}{r}\overline{p}_{r}+\frac{27M^{2}p_{t}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg)\frac{p_{t}+\xi(r)p_{r^{*}}}{\Omega^{2}p_{t}}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}

\displaystyle=\big(r\overline{\partial}_{r}-\overline{p}_{r}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}\big)-\frac{M\overline{p}_{r}}{r\Omega^{2}}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}-\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|\xi(r)|^{2}(|p_{t}|^{2}-|p_{r^{*}}|^{2})}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{4}p_{t}}\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}-\bigg(\frac{r^{\frac{1}{2}}|\xi(r)|^{2}p_{r^{*}}\overline{p}_{r}}{|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}p_{t}}+\frac{27M^{2}\xi(r)p_{r^{*}}}{r^{\frac{3}{2}}|r+6M|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{2}}\bigg)\overline{\partial}_{\overline{p}_{r}}.

The result then follows from

\displaystyle\xi(r)|p_{t}|^{2}-\xi(r)|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}+\frac{27M^{2}}{r^{2}}\Omega^{2}p_{r^{*}}p_{t}

\displaystyle=p_{t}(\xi(r)p_{t}+p_{r^{*}})-p_{t}p_{r^{*}}-\xi(r)|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}+\frac{27M^{2}}{r^{2}}\Omega^{2}p_{r^{*}}p_{t}

\displaystyle=p_{t}(\xi(r)p_{t}+p_{r^{*}})-\xi(r)|p_{r^{*}}|^{2}-|\xi(r)|^{2}p_{r^{*}}p_{t}

\displaystyle=p_{t}(\xi(r)p_{t}+p_{r^{*}})-\xi(r)p_{r^{*}}(\xi(r)p_{t}+p_{r^{*}})

\displaystyle=\Omega^{2}p_{t}\overline{p}_{r}-\Omega^{2}\xi(r)p_{r^{*}}\overline{p}_{r}.

∎
